## Act I / 第一幕

===Original===
Scene.﻿—The Exchange Coffee House in Philadelphia, a morning in August, during Washington’s first administration. It is a great room with low ceiling and neatly sanded floor. Against wall, back, are cupboards with shining pewter tankards and dishes. Centre, a great fireplace with wide stone hearth, and high-backed settles on each side. Running up right, table with chairs left of it at irregular intervals. Long seat right of table. Right, back, tub stands on floor and contains melons, cucumbers, bottles of wine and a pitcher of milk, cooling. Over fireplace is a large crimson silk liberty cap, with these words above in large letters, “Sacred to Liberty.” On wall near is the following, in large print: “Breakfast﻿—two shillings (fifty cents). Dinner, with grog or toddy﻿—three shillings (seventy-five cents). Quart of Toddy﻿—one and six (thirty-six cents). Bottle of Porter﻿—two and six (sixty cents). Best Madeira﻿—six shillings a quart (one dollar, fifty).” Entrances upper right and left, also door L. 2 E.
===Chinese===
场景——费城交易咖啡馆，华盛顿首届政府时期，八月的一个早晨。这是一间大屋子，天花板低矮，地面上洁净地铺着细沙。后墙靠墙立着一排食橱，摆着锃亮的白镩大酒杯和碟盘。正中是一座大壁炉，石砌的炉床宽大，两侧各设高背靠椅。右侧斜向上首摆着一张长桌，桌左零零散散、间隔不等地放着几把椅子，桌右有一条长凳。右后方地上立着一只大木桶，桶里冰着甜瓜、黄瓜、几瓶葡萄酒和一壶牛奶。壁炉上方悬着一顶深红丝质的自由帽，帽子上方以大字题着「献给自由」。近旁墙上贴着大字价目：「早餐——两先令（五十美分）。正餐，含格罗格酒或托迪酒——三先令（七十五美分）。托迪酒一夸脱——一先令六便士（三十六美分）。波特酒一瓶——两先令六便士（六十美分）。上等马德拉——每夸脱六先令（一美元五十美分）。」入口在右上与左上，另有左侧第二道门。

===Original===
**Reynolds**：*（Standing on chair at upper end table R.）* I’m selling, I’m selling.

**First Man**：*（Seated R. of table R. down stage end.）* Keep quiet there, will you?

**Reynolds**：*（Loudly.）* I’m selling, I’m selling. States’ securities for the price of printing. I’m selling﻿—I’m selling!

**First Man**：Can’t hear ourselves speak.

**Reynolds**：What’s the use of keeping quiet? Where’s a gentleman to do business if he can’t do it at the Exchange Coffee House?

**Second Man**：*（Seated down R. other side of table.）* Oh﻿—where’s the gentleman?
===Chinese===
**雷诺兹**：*（站在右侧上端那张桌旁的椅子上）* 卖啦，卖啦！

**男甲**：*（坐于右桌前端的右侧）* 安静点儿，行不行？

**雷诺兹**：*（高声）* 卖啦，卖啦！各州证券贱卖，只收个印刷工本价。卖啦——卖啦！

**男甲**：吵得我们连自己说话都听不见。

**雷诺兹**：安安静静的有什么用？绅士要谈买卖，不在交易咖啡馆谈，还能上哪儿去谈？

**男乙**：*（坐于右桌前端的另一侧）* 哦——绅士在哪儿呢？

===Original===
**First Man**：Send the gentleman here.

**Reynolds**：I’m the gentleman. *（Laughter.）* I’m selling, I’m selling!

**Men**：Sit down.

**First Man**：Melissa, a tankard of porter, my dear.

**Reynolds**：I’m selling, I’m selling!

**First Man**：Sit down, Reynolds! How can you be selling when there are no buyers! *（Enter **Tallyrand** up L. at back.）* Ask that gentleman if he’s nearly finished with the newspaper, Melissa.
===Chinese===
**男甲**：把那位绅士请过来。

**雷诺兹**：我就是那位绅士。*（众人哄笑）* 卖啦，卖啦！

**众人**：坐下吧。

**男甲**：梅利莎，我的好姑娘，来一大杯波特酒。

**雷诺兹**：卖啦，卖啦！

**男甲**：坐下吧，雷诺兹！连个买主都没有，你卖什么卖！*（塔列朗自后部左上登场）* 梅利莎，去问问那位先生，报纸看完了没有。

===Original===
**Reynolds**：*（Coming down stage C.）* Don’t any of you gentlemen want to make easy money? I’m offering you states’ securities for the price of the printing. Here’s a hundred dollars going to the highest bidder. Will anyone bid five? *（Holding up a paper.）*

**First Man**：What state is it on?

**Reynolds**：South Carolina. *（Laughter from the crowd.）*

**First Man**：I’ll give you sixpence for it. *（Louder laughter.）*
===Chinese===
**雷诺兹**：*（走到台中靠前）* 诸位绅士，有哪位想轻轻松松发财的？我这儿有各州证券，只收个印刷工本价。这儿是一张一百美元的票子，价高者得。有人肯出五块吗？*（举起一张纸）*

**男甲**：是哪个州的？

**雷诺兹**：南卡罗来纳。*（人群中一阵哄笑）*

**男甲**：我出六便士买这张。*（笑声更响）*

===Original===
**Giles**：*（Seated R. of round table, down stage L.）* Where’d you get all this paper all of a sudden, Reynolds?

**Reynolds**：*（R. C. down stage.）* I got it from the soldiers, Mr. Giles. The poor soldiers have entrusted me﻿—

**Giles**：Huh! They’ll be very poor soldiers if they trust you. *（General laugh.）*

**Reynolds**：*（Reading from a second paper.）* Here’s six months’ pay due Private Hiram Mott﻿—ninety-six dollars due from the state of Virginia.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：*（坐在左前圆桌的右侧）* 雷诺兹，你这一大沓纸票子，是打哪儿突然弄来的？

**雷诺兹**：*（台中偏右，靠前）* 从大兵们手里来的，贾尔斯先生。可怜的大兵们信得过我，托付给我——

**贾尔斯**：哼！他们要信了你，可就真成可怜的大兵了。*（满堂大笑）*

**雷诺兹**：*（念第二张纸）* 这是一等兵海勒姆·莫特六个月的饷银——弗吉尼亚州欠他九十六块钱。

===Original===
**Monroe**：*（Up stage L.）* That’s not true, sir; Virginia’s paid every cent she owes.

**Reynolds**：Ah, it’s easy to see you come from Virginia, sir, but the poor soldiers﻿—

**Giles**：Poor soldiers! It’s the fortune of war.
===Chinese===
**门罗**：*（左后）* 这不是真的，先生。弗吉尼亚欠下的账，一分一厘都付清了。

**雷诺兹**：啊，一听就知道您是弗吉尼亚来的，先生。可那些可怜的大兵——

**贾尔斯**：可怜的大兵！那是战争碰上的倒霉事。

===Original===
**Tallyrand**：*（He has been settling the score with **Melissa** up stage. He speaks with a slight French accent.）* Ha! Ha! The fortune of war! You bring about the war, your soldiers fight for you and conquer your enemies and then you repudiate their claim for pay. The fortune of war! The war for them﻿—the fortune for you, eh?

**Monroe**：Well, Tallyrand, I thought you had decided to return to France. Changed your mind, eh? Rather risky yet for the aristocrats to go back.

**Tallyrand**：I leave tomorrow. I am settling my score with the beautiful Melissa.

**Monroe**：*（Sitting L. of table.）* You’re going to take the chance, eh? Well, I hope to follow you in a very short time.

**Tallyrand**：*（Leaving **Melissa**, who curtseys and goes out up R.）* To follow me, Senator Monroe?
===Chinese===
**塔列朗**：*（他一直在台后同梅利莎结账。说话略带法国口音）* 哈！哈！战争的倒霉事！你们挑起战争，士兵替你们打仗，替你们征服敌人，到头来你们却赖掉他们的饷银。战争的倒霉事！仗是他们打的——财是你们发的，嗯？

**门罗**：嗬，塔列朗，我还当您已经决定回法国去了。改主意了，嗯？眼下贵族们回去，怕还是有些冒险吧。

**塔列朗**：我明天动身。我这正跟漂亮的梅利莎结账呢。

**门罗**：*（在左桌旁坐下）* 您要碰碰这个运气，嗯？好啊，但愿我不久也能跟着您去。

**塔列朗**：*（离开梅利莎，梅利莎行屈膝礼，自右上退场）* 跟我去，门罗参议员？

===Original===
**Monroe**：As Minister to France.

**Tallyrand**：*（Back of table L.）* Ah, yes, how charming! President Washington has already appointed you, eh?

**Giles**：Not yet, but we shall get it all right.

**Tallyrand**：We? Oh, you also? Two ministers?

**Giles**：No, not two ministers, but it sometimes takes two men to get one job.
===Chinese===
**门罗**：去做驻法国公使。

**塔列朗**：*（走到左桌后）* 啊，是啊，多美的事！华盛顿总统已经任命您了？

**贾尔斯**：还没有，不过我们准能拿下来。

**塔列朗**：我们？哦，您也算一个？两位公使？

**贾尔斯**：不，不是两位公使。可有的时候，办成一件事得靠两个人。

===Original===
**Tallyrand**：Ah! You mean it is not so easy. You have to deal with Alexander Hamilton.

**Giles**：We’ll deal with him all right. Alexander Hamilton﻿—

**Monroe**：Shh!

**Schuyler**：Howdy, everybody!

**Tallyrand**：*（Goes to him effusively.）* Ah, General Schuyler, how are you?

**Schuyler**：*（Turning and bowing.）* Count Tallyrand.
===Chinese===
**塔列朗**：啊！您是说，这事不那么容易。你们要对付的，是亚历山大·汉密尔顿。

**贾尔斯**：对付他，我们自有办法。亚历山大·汉密尔顿——

**门罗**：嘘！

**斯凯勒**：各位好啊！

**塔列朗**：*（热络地迎上前去）* 啊，斯凯勒将军，您好！

**斯凯勒**：*（转身还礼）* 塔列朗伯爵。

===Original===
**Tallyrand**：And how fares your illustrious son-in-law, Alexander Hamilton?

**Schuyler**：Why, I guess he’s all right. I’ve just come from Albany. I’ve been over to his house and find he’s not at home.

**Tallyrand**：Ah, ’tis good for him to get away from his labors sometimes.

**Schuyler**：Well, it’s a queer thing for him to be away this time in the morning. *（Turning again to look around to the R. another man rises and says: “Howdy, General.”）* I thought he might be here﻿—

**Reynolds**：*（Advancing effusively, taking off his hat and snaking a low bow.）* General Schuyler, now I’ll sell you one of these﻿—
===Chinese===
**塔列朗**：您那位大名鼎鼎的女婿亚历山大·汉密尔顿，近来可好？

**斯凯勒**：哦，想来还不错。我刚从奥尔巴尼来，去他府上看了一趟，他不在家。

**塔列朗**：啊，人嘛，偶尔撇下案牍歇一歇，也是好事。

**斯凯勒**：嗐，可这么大清早不着家，可就是桩新鲜事了。*（又转身朝右张望，另一名男子起身招呼：「将军，您好啊。」）* 我还当他多半在这儿——

**雷诺兹**：*（满脸堆笑地凑上前，摘下帽子，深深一躬到地）* 斯凯勒将军，我这就卖给您一张——

===Original===
**Schuyler**：Everybody calls here.

**Tallyrand**：Yes, everybody comes to see everybody at the Exchange Coffee House. I find it amusing. It is Club, Restaurant, Merchants’ Exchange, everything.

**Schuyler**：Ah, we’ll alter all that in time. Count Tallyrand. We’re young, you know. Give Alexander Hamilton time to sow some seeds. We’ll have a real Merchants’ Exchange and a real live country that will be able to pay its debts. *（Twinge of gout. **Melissa** gets chair from in front of post R. and brings it down R. of **Schuyler**, replacing it against table R., then taking tray with other drinks across to table down L., giving one to **Giles** and one to **Monroe**.）* And I hope I shall have a real foot, which I haven’t at this moment. And if you don’t mind, Melissa, my girl, I’ll just rest it in the parlor before I hobble along. Good day to you. Count Tallyrand.

**Tallyrand**：I shall call on Alexander before I leave.

**Schuyler**：He’ll be extremely glad to see you. *（Twinge.）* Confound this foot. *（Goes off R. 2 E.）*
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：人人都爱往这儿凑。

**塔列朗**：是啊，谁都得来交易咖啡馆见谁。我瞧着挺有意思。这儿是俱乐部，是饭馆，是商人交易所，什么都占全了。

**斯凯勒**：啊，这些往后都会改观的，塔列朗伯爵。要知道，我们还年轻着呢。给亚历山大·汉密尔顿点儿时间撒种。我们会有一个像样的商人交易所，会有一个还得起债的、活生生的国家。*（痛风乍然一痛。梅利莎把右柱前的椅子搬来放在斯凯勒右首，又把椅子挪回右桌边，随后端起放着另外几杯酒的托盘送到左前的桌旁，一杯给贾尔斯，一杯给门罗。）* 但愿我也有一只像样的脚——这会儿这只可算不上。梅利莎，好姑娘，你要是不嫌弃，我就先到客厅里搁一搁这只脚，再一瘸一拐地上路。祝您日安，塔列朗伯爵。

**塔列朗**：动身之前，我要去拜访一下亚历山大。

**斯凯勒**：他见了您准高兴得很。*（又是一阵剧痛）* 这只该死的脚。*（自右二道门下）*

===Original===
**Reynolds**：*（Who has been drinking and conversing with one or two shady-looking characters, coming to him.）* Count Tallyrand, before you return to la belle France, wouldn’t you like to buy up the whole of the French Loan? I’ll sell it to you for ten cents.

**Jefferson**：*（Coming down stage C.）* Good day to you, Citizens.

**Melissa**：*（Coming forward with a curtsey to L. of **Jefferson**.）* Good day to you, Mr. Jefferson.

**Jefferson**：Good day to *you*, Melissa. And how is your father today? Mending, I hope.

**Melissa**：Yes, Your Honor. Thank you, Your Honor.

**Jefferson**：No “Honor” for me; just plain Citizen, Melissa. Tell him I asked after him.
===Chinese===
**雷诺兹**：*（他一直在喝酒，有一两个面相不三不四的家伙凑过来同他搭话）* 塔列朗伯爵，回您那美丽的法兰西之前，不想把法国贷款一股脑儿买下来吗？十美分，我全卖给您。

**杰斐逊**：*（走到台中靠前）* 诸位公民，日安。

**梅利莎**：*（上前行屈膝礼，立在杰斐逊左侧）* 杰斐逊先生，日安。

**杰斐逊**：也祝*你*日安，梅利莎。你父亲今日可好些了？但愿见起色。

**梅利莎**：是，大人。谢谢您，大人。

**杰斐逊**：别跟我来什么「大人」；就叫公民，梅利莎。替我问候他。

===Original===
**Melissa**：Thank you, sir. *（Curtseys and is going R.）*

**Jefferson**：*（Turning slightly, raising finger in protest.）* Leave off the “Sir.”

**Melissa**：Yes, sir.

**Jefferson**：*（Laughs slightly amused.）* A little Madeira now, Melissa. *（She goes hurriedly R., and exits.）* Citizen Monroe﻿—*（Shakes hands.）* Good day to you, Citizen Giles. *（Does not shake with **Giles**.）*
===Chinese===
**梅利莎**：谢谢您，先生。*（行屈膝礼，正要往右去）*

**杰斐逊**：*（微微侧身，抬手示意）* 「先生」也免了。

**梅利莎**：是，先生。

**杰斐逊**：*（微觉好笑）* 这就来一小杯马德拉酒吧，梅利莎。*（她匆匆往右下去，出场）* 公民门罗——*（握手）* 日安，公民贾尔斯。*（不与贾尔斯握手）*

===Original===
**Giles**：Howdy, Tom Jefferson.

**Jefferson**：*（Turning in front of table L.）* Ah, Citizen Tallyrand.

**Tallyrand**：*（Coming down C.）* *Count* Tallyrand, if you please.

**Jefferson**：In America there are no titles. Citizen Tallyrand. In this land of the free all men are equal.

**Giles**：And they say titles ain’t so very popular in France just now.

**Jefferson**：*（Sitting R. of table L.）* Ah, France! What a glorious change! The apostle of Liberty and Fraternity.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：你好哇，汤姆·杰斐逊。

**杰斐逊**：*（转身到左桌前）* 啊，塔列朗公民。

**塔列朗**：*（走到台中）* 劳驾，是塔列朗*伯爵*。

**杰斐逊**：在美国，没有头衔。塔列朗公民。在这片自由的土地上，人人平等。

**贾尔斯**：人家还说，眼下的法兰西，头衔也不怎么吃得开呢。

**杰斐逊**：*（在左桌右侧坐下）* 啊，法兰西！多么壮丽的变革！自由与博爱的使徒。

===Original===
**Tallyrand**：Liberty! Fraternity! What do you mean by liberty, Mr. Jefferson?

**Jefferson**：*（Points to cap over mantel.）* Ask your own countrymen, Citizen Tallyrand. There you behold the symbol of the liberty of your great land of France. To us in America, that crimson cap stands as a symbol of freedom﻿—a symbol﻿—

**Tallyrand**：You call that dirty rag a symbol of Liberty? I call it a symbol of license, of lawlessness, of murder. What say you, Thomas Jefferson, to the murder of my king, Louis of France? Is that, too, a symbol of Liberty, of Fraternity?
===Chinese===
**塔列朗**：自由！博爱！杰斐逊先生，您所谓的自由，究竟是什么？

**杰斐逊**：*（指壁炉上方的帽子）* 问问您自己的同胞吧，塔列朗公民。您瞧那儿——那就是您那伟大的法兰西之自由的标志。在我们美国人看来，那顶深红的帽子是自由的象征——一个象征——

**塔列朗**：你们管那块脏破布叫自由的象征？我管它叫放纵的象征、无法无天的象征、谋杀的象征。托马斯·杰斐逊，对我那惨遭杀害的国王、法兰西的路易，您又怎么说？那也算自由的象征、博爱的象征吗？

===Original===
**Jefferson**：It is the will of the people. The time is not far distant, Citizen Tallyrand, when every king in Europe will have been swept into the dust heap of history. *（Approval from the crowd.）*

**Monroe**：Louis the Sixteenth was an oppressor of the people.

**Jefferson**：A tyrant.

**Giles**：Yes, sir.

**Tallyrand**：*（To **Jefferson**.）* You say that﻿—you who for five years were Minister to France and enjoyed his friendship? You, who have sat at his table?
===Chinese===
**杰斐逊**：那是人民的意志。塔列朗公民，那一天已为期不远：欧洲的每一位国王，都将被扫进历史的垃圾堆。*（人群中一片赞同之声）*

**门罗**：路易十六是个压迫人民的人。

**杰斐逊**：是个暴君。

**贾尔斯**：说的是，先生。

**塔列朗**：*（对杰斐逊）* 这话竟出自您口——您曾做了五年驻法公使、承蒙他友谊相待的人？您，曾在他的餐桌上用过膳？

===Original===
**Jefferson**：It was necessary to use the arm of the people. Fate decreed that your newly-founded Republic should be cemented with the blood of aristocrats.

**Giles**：Down with aristocrats! Down with tyrants everywhere!

**Tallyrand**：Yes, I know your idea of liberty! Down with aristocrats! Down with everybody﻿—who is in your way!

**Giles**：I’ll tell you one thing, Citizen Tallyrand, and I’ll tell it to you *now*. There are some damned aristocrats in this country that’ll get the same treatment your king got if they don’t go careful.

**Tallyrand**：Who helped you win your freedom? The king of France.

**Giles**：Well, we ain’t goin’ t’ have any kings in this country.
===Chinese===
**杰斐逊**：那时节，不得不借助人民这条臂膀。命运注定，你们新生的共和国要用贵族的鲜血来浇固根基。

**贾尔斯**：打倒贵族！打倒天下一切的暴君！

**塔列朗**：好哇，你们那套自由我可领教了！打倒贵族！打倒一切——碍了你们道的人！

**贾尔斯**：塔列朗公民，有件事我要告诉你，*现在*就告诉你。这个国家也有那么几个该死的贵族，他们要是不放规矩点儿，落的下场就跟你们那位国王一个样。

**塔列朗**：当年是谁帮你们赢得自由的？法兰西的国王。

**贾尔斯**：哼，这个国家容不下什么国王。

===Original===
**Tallyrand**：Who desires to be king?

**Giles**：*（Fiercely.）* George Washington does.

**Monroe**：And Alexander Hamilton wants to be Prime Minister! *Wants* to be? He is Prime Minister this very minute. Prime Minister of America. Huh!

**Jefferson**：*（Conciliatory.）* I assure you, Citizen Tallyrand, Citizen Giles and Citizen Monroe voice the sentiments of the great body of the American people. *（Murmurs of assent from the crowd.）* There is a growing unrest all over this land at the aristocratic tendencies of our President. There is bitter and righteous opposition to Alexander Hamilton’s efforts to centralize the government and assume the debts of the thirteen free and independent states. Such a centralization of power would inevitably lead to monarchy. I stand on the platform of the Rights of Man﻿—the rights of the individual﻿—the right of each state to its freedom. And I tell you, Citizen Tallyrand, the gravest danger that threatens America today rests in the persons of those men who are striving to centralize the power of the United States; striving to establish a military dictatorship.
===Chinese===
**塔列朗**：谁想当国王？

**贾尔斯**：*（凶相毕露）* 乔治·华盛顿就想。

**门罗**：亚历山大·汉密尔顿还想当首相呢！何止是「想」？他此刻就已经是首相了。美利坚的首相。哼！

**杰斐逊**：*（打圆场）* 我向您保证，塔列朗公民，公民贾尔斯和公民门罗道出的，正是美国广大人民的心声。*（人群中低声附和）* 对我国总统的贵族化倾向，全国上下正日益不安。对亚历山大·汉密尔顿集中政府权力、承担十三个自由独立州债务的图谋，存在着一股激烈而正当的反对力量。这样的权力集中，不可避免地会导向君主政体。我站在人权的立场上——个人权利的立场——每一州各自自由的立场。塔列朗公民，我告诉您，今日威胁美国的最深重的危险，正落在那些竭力集中合众国权力、竭力建立军事独裁的人们身上。

===Original===
**Monroe**：A condition that will involve us in European quarrels in which it should be our policy to take no part.

**Tallyrand**：How can you keep out of European quarrels when your interests are bound up with those of Europe?

**Monroe**：Our riches and resources can bid defiance to any power on earth. It is only when our rights are invaded that we should make preparation for our defense.

**Tallyrand**：*（With a shrug.）* Yes, and then it will be perhaps too late.

**Jefferson**：Citizen Tallyrand, I look for the day when during the rage of eternal wars in Europe, the lion and the lamb within our regions shall lie down together in peace.

**Tallyrand**：*（Down beside **Jefferson**.）* Yes, they would lie down together﻿—until the lion feel hungry, then he would get up and eat the lamb.
===Chinese===
**门罗**：那样一种局面，会把我们卷进欧洲的纷争，而我们的国策本该是概不参与。

**塔列朗**：你们的利益同欧洲的利益纠缠在一起，又怎能置身事外？

**门罗**：我们的财富与资源，足以傲视天下任何强权。只有当我们的权利遭到侵犯时，才该着手整备防御。

**塔列朗**：*（耸了耸肩）* 是啊，可到那时，只怕就太迟了。

**杰斐逊**：塔列朗公民，我期待着这样一天：当欧洲还在无休止的战火中激荡，我们这片土地上的狮子与羔羊，却能相安无事地同眠。

**塔列朗**：*（在杰斐逊身旁坐下）* 是啊，它们会同眠的——等狮子饿了，起身就把羔羊吃掉。

===Original===
**Monroe**：Against this tendency toward centralization, we who love the freedom of our own state will fight to the death.

**Tallyrand**：*（Moving to C.）* Yes, thirteen jealous states all working against each other. How are you going to pay your debts without a central government? You have no credit abroad. Your paper is not worth five cents on the dollar. Why don’t you pay the men who furnished you supplies for your war? Why don’t you pay the soldiers who gained you that liberty that you love so dearly?

**Giles**：Damn the soldiers. This country’s goin’ to put a stop to Washington’s coddling of the army.

**Tallyrand**：*（Advancing a little to **Giles**.）* It was the soldiers who won you your precious freedom.

**Monroe**：Well, Virginia’s paid her soldiers.

**Tallyrand**：*（Coming down on **Monroe’s** level.）* Has South Carolina? Has Rhode Island?
===Chinese===
**门罗**：面对这种集中权力的倾向，我们这些热爱本州自由的人，誓死也要抗争到底。

**塔列朗**：*（走到台中）* 好啊，十三个彼此嫉妒的州，个个对着干。没有一个中央政府，你们的债拿什么还？你们在海外毫无信用，你们的纸票子一块钱卖不上五分。为什么不去偿付那些为你们的战争供应商粮的人？为什么不去偿付那些为你们赢得自由的士兵——你们不是把那自由爱得要命吗？

**贾尔斯**：去他的大兵。这个国家总有一天要叫华盛顿别再那么娇惯军队。

**塔列朗**：*（朝贾尔斯逼近一步）* 正是那些大兵，替你们赢得了那宝贵的自由。

**门罗**：哼，弗吉尼亚给自己的士兵发足了饷。

**塔列朗**：*（下到与门罗齐平处）* 南卡罗来纳发了吗？罗得岛呢？

===Original===
**Monroe**：That’s no affair of Virginia.

**Giles**：No, sir.

**Tallyrand**：Why not? Did not the soldiers of Rhode Island help Virginia to her liberty? Each one fought for the common good. Each one should be paid.

**Jefferson**：By that, Citizen Tallyrand, I understand you to mean that the government should assume the war debts of all the states.

**Tallyrand**：Those debts were the price of your liberty. If you have a government, it should pay the country’s debts.
===Chinese===
**门罗**：那不干弗吉尼亚的事。

**贾尔斯**：不干，先生。

**塔列朗**：怎么不干？罗得岛的士兵难道没帮弗吉尼亚赢得自由？人人都是为共同的事业战斗，人人就该得到偿付。

**杰斐逊**：塔列朗公民，我明白了——您的意思是，政府应当承担各州的战争债务。

**塔列朗**：那些债务，正是你们自由的代价。既然你们有一个政府，它就该偿还国家的债务。

===Original===
**Jefferson**：Citizen Tallyrand, you are simply speaking from Alexander Hamilton’s platform. You are an aristocrat.

**Monroe**：So’s Hamilton.

**Jefferson**：As such you cannot possibly understand the love of liberty that burns in the heart of every loyal American. *（Rising and coming to **Tallyrand**.）* This attempt by Alexander Hamilton to compel the central government to assume the debts of the thirteen states is merely a trick, a maneuver, to give greater power to that central body and to ruthlessly crush the freedom of the states. We, as Virginians, love Virginia. Her freedom﻿—We will fight for her freedom﻿—
===Chinese===
**杰斐逊**：塔列朗公民，您这套话，根本就是亚历山大·汉密尔顿的调子。您是个贵族。

**门罗**：汉密尔顿也是。

**杰斐逊**：正因如此，您才绝无可能懂得，每一个忠诚的美国人心底燃烧着怎样对自由的爱。*（起身，走向塔列朗）* 亚历山大·汉密尔顿这番胁迫中央政府承担十三州债务的举动，不过是一个伎俩、一种手段，好叫那个中央机构攫取更大的权力，好无情地碾碎各州的自由。我们身为弗吉尼亚人，热爱弗吉尼亚。她的自由——我们要为她的自由战斗——

===Original===
**Tallyrand**：*（Coming toward him.）* Fight! *Mon Dieu!* Where were you when Alexander Hamilton stormed the redoubts at Yorktown?

**Jefferson**：As you know, Citizen Tallyrand, I am not a soldier. *（Sits.）*

**Giles**：*（Winking at **Monroe**.）* Jefferson has never been a fighter, you know, Citizen Tallyrand.

**Tallyrand**：*（C.）* Citizen! Citizen! Citizen! You prate and boast about the rights of man, and sneer at Alexander Hamilton as an aristocrat. Have any of you worked for the Rights of Man as he has? When it was an affair of fighting for your liberty, he fought. At the age of nineteen﻿—twenty years, he had risen to be Colonel and was leading the victorious charge at Yorktown. At the hour when your liberty was assured he lay down his arms and commenced to make a nation of you. I tell you I have known all the great men of my time﻿—Pitt, Fox, Washington﻿—and of them all it is my boast that I know Alexander Hamilton. Adieu!
===Chinese===
**塔列朗**：*（朝他走近）* 战斗！我的天！亚历山大·汉密尔顿在约克镇攻夺堡垒的时候，您在哪儿？

**杰斐逊**：您是知道的，塔列朗公民，我并非行伍中人。*（坐下）*

**贾尔斯**：*（冲门罗挤挤眼）* 杰斐逊从来就不是个斗士，这您知道的，塔列朗公民。

**塔列朗**：*（台中）* 公民！公民！公民！你们夸夸其谈什么人权，却讥笑亚历山大·汉密尔顿是贵族。你们中间，有谁像他那样为人权出过力？到该为你们的自由打仗的时候，他打了。十九岁——不满二十岁，他已晋升上校，在约克镇率队冲锋、克敌制胜。你们的自由大局已定之时，他放下刀枪，着手把你们造就成一个国家。告诉你们，我一生识遍了当代的伟人——皮特、福克斯、华盛顿——而他们之中，我最引以为傲的，是结识了亚历山大·汉密尔顿。告辞！

===Original===
**Giles**：Why, the crazy﻿—

**Jefferson**：Hush! He’s right, friend, he’s right. Hamilton is a great man, but his energies are misdirected.

**Giles**：Great man! Why, he ain’t got half the following you have!

**Monroe**：That infernal French aristocrat has put the whole thing in a nutshell. Hamilton and Washington are working against the interests of the individual. They’re working against us.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：嗬，这个疯——

**杰斐逊**：嘘！他说得对，朋友，他说得对。汉密尔顿是位伟人，只可惜精力用错了地方。

**贾尔斯**：伟人！嗐，他手底下追随的人，连您的一半都不到！

**门罗**：那个可恶的法国贵族，一句话把事情全说穿了。汉密尔顿和华盛顿在跟各个人的利益作对，也就是在同我们作对。

===Original===
**Jefferson**：Come, come, we mustn’t say that.

**Monroe**：Well, it’s true!

**Jefferson**：That may be, but﻿—

**Giles**：Well, what you goin’ t’ do about it!

**Jefferson**：*（To **Melissa**.）* Ah, thank you, my dear. *（He and **Monroe** drink. As they drink **Reynolds** flirts with **Melissa**; see above.）* Citizen Monroe, I came in the hope of finding you here this morning because I have decided that it is necessary that you and I should make a friendly call upon Alexander Hamilton.
===Chinese===
**杰斐逊**：得了，得了，这话我们可不能说。

**门罗**：哼，可这是实话！

**杰斐逊**：也许是吧，不过——

**贾尔斯**：那你们打算怎么办！

**杰斐逊**：*（对梅利莎）* 啊，谢谢你，亲爱的。*（他与门罗饮酒。两人喝酒之际，雷诺兹在同梅利莎调情；见前）* 公民门罗，今儿早晨我原是抱着能在这儿碰见您的希望来的，因为我已经拿定主意：您我二人，该去对亚历山大·汉密尔顿作一次友好拜访。

===Original===
**Monroe**：A friendly call!

**Jefferson**：To conciliate him.

**Monroe**：With what object?

**Jefferson**：We need his cooperation. *（Sits again.）* The decision of the location of the Capital of the United States is now a matter of urgent necessity. It is vitally necessary that we should secure the Capital for the South, where our influence is paramount.
===Chinese===
**门罗**：友好拜访！

**杰斐逊**：去安抚他。

**门罗**：所为何来？

**杰斐逊**：我们需要他的合作。*（重新坐下）* 合众国首都定于何处，如今已是燃眉之急。首都务必拿到南方去，那才是我们影响力最大的地方。

===Original===
**Monroe**：Don’t see any need to worry about that. The North hasn’t got any chance anyway. Why, Washington’s a Virginian﻿—if he is under Alexander Hamilton’s thumb.

**Giles**：*（Rising.）* George Washington ain’t got a damned bit of loyalty in him!

**Jefferson**：Come, come! I cannot discuss this matter with you, Citizen Giles, unless you refrain from invective.

**Giles**：Well﻿—he’s a Virginian, and yet he is just as interested in New York and Massachusetts as he is in Virginia. It makes me sick.

**Monroe**：The Capital of the United States doesn’t go to the North as long as James Monroe has a fight in him.

**Jefferson**：Let us consider our own position. Hamilton is straining every nerve to pass through Congress his Bill for the Government Assumption of States’ Debts.
===Chinese===
**门罗**：瞧不出有什么可愁的。北方横竖没有指望。嗐，华盛顿自己就是弗吉尼亚人——哪怕他被亚历山大·汉密尔顿攥在手心里。

**贾尔斯**：*（起身）* 乔治·华盛顿身上找不出半点儿该死的忠心！

**杰斐逊**：得了，得了！公民贾尔斯，您若不收起这种谩骂，我就无法同您讨论此事。

**贾尔斯**：好吧——他是弗吉尼亚人，可他对纽约和马萨诸塞上的那份心，竟同对弗吉尼亚的一模一样。叫我恶心。

**门罗**：只要我詹姆斯·门罗还有一口气斗下去，合众国的首都就落不到北方。

**杰斐逊**：先掂量掂量我们自己的处境。汉密尔顿正竭尽全力，要使国会通过他那项由政府承担各州债务的州债法案。

===Original===
**Giles**：It’s an outrage. Let every state pay its own debts.

**Monroe**：Well, we’re blocking that bill, and will continue to block it to the last ditch.

**Jefferson**：Without our cooperation﻿—which we cannot possibly extend﻿—his bill cannot go through.

**Monroe**：Then what’s the use of us going to ask favors of him when he knows perfectly well that we are the most active opponents of his bill?
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：简直是岂有此理。各州的债，就该各州自己还。

**门罗**：哼，我们正卡着那项法案，还要一直卡到底，拼到最后一步。

**杰斐逊**：没有我们的合作——这一层我们万万给不了——他的法案就通不过。

**门罗**：那还上赶着去求他做什么？他心里明镜似的，知道我们是他的法案最卖力的对头。

===Original===
**Giles**：I say, fight him.

**Monroe**：I believe you’re right, Giles, fight him!

**Jefferson**：*（Coming toward table L.）* Come, come, Monroe, more flies may be caught with a dish of molasses than with a sea of vinegar. You know you’ve set your heart on being appointed Minister to France.

**Monroe**：I have.

**Jefferson**：Hamilton’s word will go a long way with Washington. *（**Reynolds** turns his head slightly toward them, then picking up paper sits on seat R. of fireplace and reads, removing his hat, which he places beside him.）* Come, we’ll make a friendly call.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：依我说，跟他斗。

**门罗**：贾尔斯，我看你说得对。跟他斗！

**杰斐逊**：*（走向左桌）* 得了，得了，门罗，一盘糖浆捉住的苍蝇，比一海子醋捉住的还多。你知道，你一心惦着被任命为驻法公使呢。

**门罗**：不假。

**杰斐逊**：汉密尔顿的一句话，在华盛顿跟前顶得上大用场。*（雷诺兹把头微微朝他们一偏，随后拿起一张纸，坐到壁炉右侧的座位上读起来，摘下帽子放在身旁）* 走吧，我们作一次友好拜访去。

===Original===
**Giles**：Hamilton will fight tooth and nail to have the Capital in New York.

**Jefferson**：That may be. Hamilton is a New Yorker.

**Giles**：*（Sneers.）* Is he? He comes from God knows where.

**Jefferson**：Sh﻿—sh!

**Giles**：A bastard, born in the﻿—

**Monroe**：We don’t need to discuss his arrival into the world, Giles. I am far more interested in his removal.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：汉密尔顿会拼死拼活把首都留在纽约的。

**杰斐逊**：那也难说。汉密尔顿是纽约人嘛。

**贾尔斯**：*（嗤笑）* 是吗？他呀，天知道是打哪儿冒出来的。

**杰斐逊**：嘘——嘘！

**贾尔斯**：一个野种，生在——

**门罗**：他是怎么降临到这世上来的，咱们用不着讨论，贾尔斯。我更感兴趣的是怎么把他从这世上请出去。

===Original===
**Jefferson**：*（Moving to C.）* We must be prepared for his opposition to the South﻿—

**Monroe**：It will be a lasting disgrace to this country if the Capital is not in Virginia.

**Jefferson**：Too remote, Monroe. You see we’ve no post-roads. Inaccessible from New England.

**Giles**：Damn it! Ain’t you workin’ for Virginia?

**Jefferson**：I’m afraid we cannot hope for Virginia. I believe, though, if we go carefully, there is a chance of getting it for the South.
===Chinese===
**杰斐逊**：*（走向台中）* 他必定反对定都南方，我们得有个准备——

**门罗**：首都要是定不到弗吉尼亚，那这个国家可就丢尽了脸。

**杰斐逊**：太偏远了，门罗。你也知道，我们连驿道都没有，从新英格兰根本进不去。

**贾尔斯**：见鬼！你不是替弗吉尼亚办事的吗？

**杰斐逊**：弗吉尼亚恐怕是指望不上了。不过我相信，只要走得稳当，把首都拿到南方，还是有机会的。

===Original===
**Monroe**：Where?

**Jefferson**：On the Potomac.

**Giles**：Well, that’s a damned sight better’n New York.

**Jefferson**：*（Coming back to table.）* Come, we will call on Hamilton this evening﻿—a friendly call﻿—after supper, perhaps. But remember we must steer clear of any mention of his Bill for the Government Assumption of States’ Debts.
===Chinese===
**门罗**：拿到哪儿？

**杰斐逊**：波托马克河。

**贾尔斯**：哼，那可比纽约强出一大截。

**杰斐逊**：*（回到桌边）* 来吧，今晚我们就去拜访汉密尔顿——友好拜访——晚饭之后吧，大概。可要记住，千万避开别提他那项政府承担州债的法案。

===Original===
**Monroe**：It doesn’t suit me to go begging to Hamilton.

**Giles**：He’s got the President wound round his little finger.

**Monroe**：And the people trust him.

**Giles**：Shake the people’s faith in him, that’s the thing.

**Jefferson**：He’s honest, Giles. We’ve tested his honesty.

**Monroe**：Yes, the Anti-Federalists have attacked his honesty as Secretary of the Treasury from every possible angle, and he’s always beaten us.
===Chinese===
**门罗**：我可不愿意上汉密尔顿门上去乞求。

**贾尔斯**：总统都被他玩弄得团团转。

**门罗**：老百姓还信着他呢。

**贾尔斯**：动摇老百姓对他的信任，这才是正经事。

**杰斐逊**：他是清白的，贾尔斯。他的清白，我们检验过。

**门罗**：是啊，反联邦党人从一切可能的角度攻击过他当财政部长的操守，回回都叫他打败了。

===Original===
**Giles**：We ain’t used up our whole bag o’ tricks yet, not by a damned sight.

**Jefferson**：*（With a slight look of disapproval at **Giles**.）* Then, Citizen Monroe, we will meet here this evening at nine. *（**Monroe** rises.）* We will make a late call on Hamilton, as I wish our visit to be regarded scarcely as one relating to business.

**Giles**：*（Scratching chin.）* If we could only make the people believe that Hamilton, the Secretary of the Treasury, ain’t playin’ fair with the gate-money﻿—why, we’d have the hull country in our pockets.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：我们那袋子招数还没抖搂完呢，差得远呢。

**杰斐逊**：*（对贾尔斯微露不以为然之色）* 那么，公民门罗，今晚九点我们在此会齐。*（门罗起身）* 我们很晚才去拜访汉密尔顿，正因为我希望这次拜访看起来压根不像谈公事的。

**贾尔斯**：*（挠着下巴）* 只要能让老百姓相信，财政部长汉密尔顿在银钱上不干净——嘿，那整个国家就都装进咱们口袋了。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：I think I’ll be hobbling off, Melissa. My regards to your father.

**Jefferson**：Good day to you. Citizen Schuyler.

**Schuyler**：*（At post L. of fireplace.）* Howdy do, Mr. Jefferson. Howdy do, Senator Monroe.

**Jefferson**：And how’s the gout?

**Schuyler**：Well, it’s﻿—*（Gets a twinge.）* Oh﻿—I can’t tell you in the presence of this young lady.
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：梅利莎，我看我也该一瘸一拐地上路了。替我问候你父亲。

**杰斐逊**：祝您日安，斯凯勒公民。

**斯凯勒**：*（在壁炉左侧柱旁）* 你好啊，杰斐逊先生。你好啊，门罗参议员。

**杰斐逊**：痛风怎么样了？

**斯凯勒**：嗐，这个——*（一阵剧痛）* 哦——有这位年轻姑娘在场，我实在说不出口。

===Original===
**Monroe**：*（Coming up L.）* We were just talking about your son-in-law, Alexander Hamilton.

**Schuyler**：*（With a grunt.）* Ah! That doesn’t surprise me.

**Jefferson**：Mrs. Hamilton is not back from England yet?

**Schuyler**：No, Betsy’s not back yet.

**Jefferson**：I understand she went over to see your other daughter who was sick. I trust she is better.

**Schuyler**：Yes, she’s all right now, thank God!
===Chinese===
**门罗**：*（走向左上）* 我们方才正说起您那位女婿，亚历山大·汉密尔顿。

**斯凯勒**：*（哼了一声）* 啊！这我可不奇怪。

**杰斐逊**：汉密尔顿夫人还没从英格兰回来？

**斯凯勒**：没有，贝特西还没回来。

**杰斐逊**：听说她是去看您另一位患病的女儿了。但愿她已经好转。

**斯凯勒**：好了，她没事了，感谢上帝！

===Original===
**Monroe**：Mr. Jefferson and I propose to drop in and see Alexander Hamilton tonight.

**Jefferson**：I hope that he is well.

**Schuyler**：Yes, he’s as well as you fellows will let him be.

**Monroe**：*（A step toward him angrily.）* What do you mean by that, General Schuyler?

**Schuyler**：*（Responding with anger.）* You know what I mean. *（**Monroe** goes up to street door L.）*
===Chinese===
**门罗**：杰斐逊先生和我打算今晚顺道去看看亚历山大·汉密尔顿。

**杰斐逊**：望他安好。

**斯凯勒**：好着呢——好到你们这帮人容得下的地步。

**门罗**：*（恼怒地抢上一步）* 将军，您这话是什么意思？

**斯凯勒**：*（也动了火）* 你们知道我是什么意思。*（门罗走向左侧临街的门）*

===Original===
**Jefferson**：*（Between them and laughing.）* Come, come, do not let us quarrel. We’re coming in to have a little chat with Citizen Hamilton concerning the location of the Capital. *（**Schuyler** looks interested.）* Citizen Schuyler, I bid you good day, sir. Good day to you, Citizen Giles.

**Monroe**：Good day.

**Giles**：Good day. *（Exit **Jefferson** and **Monroe** to L. through street door.）* Give my love to Alexander.
===Chinese===
**杰斐逊**：*（隔在两人中间，笑着）* 得了，得了，别伤了和气。我们是来同公民汉密尔顿聊聊首都选址的事的。*（斯凯勒面露关注）* 斯凯勒公民，我祝您日安。也祝您日安，公民贾尔斯。

**门罗**：日安。

**贾尔斯**：日安。*（杰斐逊与门罗自左侧临街门下）* 替我向亚历山大问好。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：*（Looks after **Jefferson** and **Monroe** and then at **Giles.**）* You’re cooking something for him between you, and you’re the chief stoker.

**Giles**：I’m a fighter, if that’s what you mean.

**Schuyler**：Yes, you’re a fighter, but a damned poor sportsman. When your party wants to circulate any damnable insinuations about Alexander Hamilton, they go to Giles of Virginia and he does the dirty work. *（**Giles** turns.）* Because you and your gang know that Alexander has the confidence of the people and that he means to make the government assume the states’ debts, you are forever trying to trip him up﻿—shouting corruption in the Treasury, dishonesty in the Treasury, and God knows what. *（Moving toward L. and turning again.）* I don’t say who’s the author of the accusations. I don’t say it’s Tom Jefferson or Senator Monroe, but I know where to put my hand on the man who does the dirty work. *（**Giles** crosses angrily toward him.）* Yes, you’re a fighter, but you know only one knockout blow, and that’s the one below the belt. You needn’t glare at me. I wouldn’t soil my hands with you, but this is one of the times when I wish I had gout anywhere but in my foot.
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：*（目送杰斐逊与门罗出去，又看看贾尔斯）* 你们几个正合起伙来整治他呢，你呀，就是那个烧火的头子。

**贾尔斯**：您要是指这个，那我承认，我是个好斗的。

**斯凯勒**：是，你是个好斗的，可你是个糟糕透顶的运动家。你们那伙人一想散布什么有关亚历山大·汉密尔顿的下流流言，就去找弗吉尼亚的贾尔斯，脏活儿就归他干。*（贾尔斯转过身去）* 就因为你们和你那帮人清楚，亚历山大得着人民的信任，清楚他铁了心要让政府承担各州债务，你们就没完没了地给他使绊子——嚷嚷什么财政部贪污啦，财政部舞弊啦，还有天知道些什么。*（走向左侧，又回过身）* 我不说那些攻讦是谁编出来的。我不说是汤姆·杰斐逊，也不说是门罗参议员，可我知道该上哪儿去找那个干脏活儿的人。*（贾尔斯怒冲冲地朝他逼来）* 是啊，你是个好斗的，可你只会一记把人打倒的绝招——照着腰带以下打的那一招。用不着瞪我。我才不愿为你这号人脏了手，只是这种时候，我真恨不得这痛风长在身上哪儿都行，别偏偏长在脚上。

===Original===
**Reynolds**：*（Who has been listening and enjoying the row, says meditatively.）* Shake the people’s faith in him.

**Giles**：*（Turning to him.）* What d’you say?

**Reynolds**：*（Smiling.）* Shake the people’s faith in him.

**Giles**：You’ve been listening, eh?

**Reynolds**：Well, that’s the only way I get a living, Mr. Giles﻿—keeping my ears open.

**Giles**：Well, keep your mouth shut.
===Chinese===
**雷诺兹**：*（他一直在旁边听着这场争吵，听得津津有味，若有所思地说）* 动摇老百姓对他的信任。

**贾尔斯**：*（转向他）* 你说什么？

**雷诺兹**：*（微笑）* 动摇老百姓对他的信任。

**贾尔斯**：你一直在偷听，嗯？

**雷诺兹**：嗐，我就靠这个营生，贾尔斯先生——耳朵支棱着。

**贾尔斯**：那就管好你那张嘴。

===Original===
**Reynolds**：*（Rising and coming to C. slowly, laughing.）* Pretty hard job to shake the people’s faith in Alexander Hamilton, ain’t it?

**Giles**：Mind your own damn business.

**Reynolds**：*（Laughs and crosses to chair back of table L.; puts his hat down on table.）* You’ve tried to prove him incompetent; you’ve tried to prove him dishonest; but there’s one thing you haven’t tried, Mr. Giles. *（Back of table.）*
===Chinese===
**雷诺兹**：*（缓缓起身，走到台中，笑着）* 要动摇老百姓对亚历山大·汉密尔顿的信任，可没那么容易，是不是？

**贾尔斯**：滚你的，少管闲事。

**雷诺兹**：*（笑着走到左桌后的椅子，把帽子搁在桌上）* 你们想证明他不称职——没成；想证明他不老实——也没成。可有一样你们还没试过，贾尔斯先生。*（走到桌后）*

===Original===
**Giles**：*（Turning away from him.）* Go to the devil.

**Reynolds**：And it’s strange you haven’t thought of it. How about a woman?

**Giles**：You’re a little gentleman, ain’t yer, Reynolds?

**Reynolds**：Women are the deuce for tangling up a man’s finances.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：*（扭头不理他）* 见你的鬼去。

**雷诺兹**：说来也怪，你们竟没想到这一招。使个女人，怎么样？

**贾尔斯**：你还真是个「体面小人儿」，是不是，雷诺兹？

**雷诺兹**：要把一个男人的钱财搅得一塌糊涂，女人最是拿手。

===Original===
**Giles**：You know all about it, don’t you?

**Reynolds**：Yes, I know a good deal about women. I’m married, you know.

**Giles**：Yes, I know.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：这里头的门道，你全懂，是吧？

**雷诺兹**：懂，女人的事我懂不少。要知道，我是结了婚的。

**贾尔斯**：这我知道。

===Original===
**Reynolds**：I know a good deal about men, too. Now Mrs. Hamilton is away, been away a long time in England. Now, what do you say﻿—

**Giles**：*（Turning away.）* I don’t want any advice from you. Besides, as you know so much, you know that Hamilton hardly ever leaves his house.

**Reynolds**：Makes it all the easier. Send the woman to the house.

**Giles**：*（Looking in front of him.）* What the devil do you mean?

**Reynolds**：It’s a matter of choosing the right woman and the right moment. *（Puts down pipe and leans forward.）* You’ve seen Mrs. Reynolds, haven’t you?
===Chinese===
**雷诺兹**：男人的事我也懂不少。眼下汉密尔顿夫人不在家，在英格兰待了好久了。那么您说——

**贾尔斯**：*（扭过头去）* 我不要你出什么主意。再说，你既然懂得这么多，你也该如何道，汉密尔顿几乎从不迈出府门。

**雷诺兹**：那就更省事了。把女人送到他府上去。

**贾尔斯**：*（直望着前方）* 你到底安的什么心？

**雷诺兹**：这不过是挑对女人、挑准时辰的事。*（放下烟斗，俯身向前）* 你见过雷诺兹夫人吧？

===Original===
**Giles**：Your wife?

**Reynolds**：Yes.

**Giles**：*（Turning squarely toward him.）* God, you’re a bad ’un, ain’t you?

**Reynolds**：*（Leaning back and smiling.）* I’m what may be called a soldier of fortune, Mr. Giles.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：你老婆？

**雷诺兹**：正是。

**贾尔斯**：*（转过身，正对着他）* 天哪，你可真是个坏种，是不是？

**雷诺兹**：*（仰身而笑）* 我这人嘛，勉强称得上个冒险家，贾尔斯先生。

===Original===
**Giles**：You come in on the blackmailing end of the game, eh? You’d do any damned thing for a ten dollar piece, wouldn’t you?

**Reynolds**：Yes, I’m afraid my price is a little lower than yours, Mr. Giles. *（Leans forward again.）* You might see Mrs. Reynolds. She’s a nice little thing. I’m very fond of her, but she’s too good for me.

**Giles**：*（Looking in front of him again.）* Oh!

**Reynolds**：Yes, it’s the clothes, you know, that cause the trouble. She must have pretty clothes. She’s young, you see. She﻿—*（Noticing that **Giles** is interested he takes a pen and writes on a slip of paper, rises and puts the paper on **Giles’** knee.）* That’s our address, Mr. Giles.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：你是干敲诈那一行的，嗯？只要给上十块钱，什么缺德事你都肯干，是不是？

**雷诺兹**：是啊，只怕我的身价，比您还低那么一点儿，贾尔斯先生。*（又俯身向前）* 您不妨见见雷诺兹夫人。她是个可人的小东西。我很疼她，只可惜她跟了我，委屈了她。

**贾尔斯**：*（又直望前方）* 哦！

**雷诺兹**：是呀，您知道，惹祸的根子全在衣裳上。她非得有漂亮衣裳穿不可。她还年轻，您瞧。她——*（见贾尔斯上了心，他取过一支笔，在一张纸条上写了几个字，起身把纸条放在贾尔斯膝上）* 那是我们家的地址，贾尔斯先生。

===Original===
**Giles**：She be at home now? *（Looking at paper.）*

**Reynolds**：*（Smiling and coming back a step.）* Yes, I think so. You’ve seen her with me, haven’t you?

**Giles**：Yes.

**Reynolds**：She’s a pretty little thing, ain’t she? *（**Giles** looks at him sideways.）* She’s a clever little thing, too. Well, good day, Mr. Giles.
===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：这会儿她在家吗？*（看着纸条）*

**雷诺兹**：*（微笑着退回一步）* 在，我想是在的。您见过她同我在一起，不是吗？

**贾尔斯**：见过。

**雷诺兹**：她是个俊俏的小东西，是不是？*（贾尔斯斜眼看他）* 还是个伶俐的小东西呢。好啦，日安，贾尔斯先生。

## Act II / 第二幕



===Original===

Scene.﻿—Living-room in the house of Alexander Hamilton, 79 South Third Street, Philadelphia. Evening of the same day. It is a large room in a brick house of the period. Wallpaper dull gray, white wainscoting. Doors solid mahogany, white frames, with cut-glass handles. Woodwork of windows and fireplace handsomely carved and painted white. Room suggests dignity and comfort rather than elegance. The furniture is Chippendale. Portraits of General and Mrs. Schuyler on the wall at back. Up stage right there is a door leading to a hall and street door which can be seen by audience. Down left a door leading to other part of house. Left center, at back, two long French windows give on to narrow iron balcony on front of house. A third window is on the right up stage. Light through window suggests street lamp without. Large and small chairs stand about. At left front stands a large carved mahogany chair. It is handsomely upholstered in leather. At left centre, a large, low writing table with a pile of manuscript at one end and furnished with ink and quill pens. The fireplace is on the right down stage. On mantelpiece stands a handsome wedgewood vase and one silver four-branch candlestick; above mantel a large mirror. Between fireplace and window up right a small square mahogany table against the wall. Green rep and lace curtains ai all windows. Down right centre facing audience a Colonial sofa. Against wall, centre at back, is a mahogany bookcase, filled with books, no doors on case; standing on bookcase are two two-branch silver candlesticks. The two portraits are on the wall above. Right and left of bookcase a chair. In left corner up stage a handsome Colonial escritoire, open. Armchair in front of escritoire. Against wall left and above door a smaller similar bookcase. Two single candlesticks on ditto. Armchair back of **Hamilton’s** table left centre. Single chair right of table. Chair against wall below door down left. Ditto against wall below fireplace down right. Small chair back of sofa.

===Chinese===

场景——费城南第三街 79 号，亚历山大·汉密尔顿家的客厅。同日晚间。这是当年一座砖宅里的大房间。墙纸暗灰，白色护墙板。房门为实心红木，门框雪白，装着雕花玻璃门把。窗与壁炉的木作雕工精美，漆成白色。整个房间透着庄重与舒适，而非华贵。家具是齐彭代尔式的。后墙上挂着斯凯勒将军夫妇的肖像。右上有一道门通向门厅和临街大门，观众可以望见。左下有一道门通向宅子其余部分。左中偏后，两扇长长的落地法式窗通向宅子正面一座窄窄的铁阳台。右上另有第三扇窗。窗外透进来的光线示意着户外的街灯。房间里散置着大小椅子。左前立着一把雕花大红木椅，皮面装裱得十分讲究。左中是一张宽大的矮写字台，一端摞着一沓手稿，备有墨水和羽笔。壁炉在右前。壁炉台上立着一只精美的韦奇伍德花瓶和一座银质四枝烛台；壁炉上方是一面大镜子。右上的壁炉与窗之间，靠墙放着一张方形小红木桌。所有窗子都挂着绿色棱纹布帘加纱帘。右中面向观众摆着一张殖民地式长榻。后墙正中靠墙是一座红木书柜，装满了书，柜子不带柜门；柜顶立着两座银质双枝烛台。那两幅肖像就挂在柜子上方的墙上。书柜左右各一把椅子。左后角是一张精致的殖民地式斜面写字台，台面敞开。写字台前一把扶手椅。左墙临门上方还有一座式样稍小的类似书柜，柜上也放着两只单枝烛台。左中汉密尔顿的桌子后一把扶手椅。桌子右侧一把单人椅。左下门下方靠墙一把椅子。右下壁炉下方靠墙同样一把。长榻后一把小椅。

===Original===

**Zekiel**：*（At door L.）* ‘Scuse me, Marse Ham’ton, but es mighty nigh nine o’clock.

**Hamilton**：Is it, Zekiel?

**Zekiel**：It cut’ny am, sah.

**Hamilton**：Well, what of it?

**Zekiel**：*（Coming toward him.）* Cunnel, yo’ ain’ had scarcely no food all day, an’ dad fetch me, dinnah’s been waitin’ these fo’ hours.

**Hamilton**：Has it? Well, bring it in here.

===Chinese===

**泽基尔**：*（在左侧门前）* 对不住，汉密尔顿老爷，可都快九点啦。

**汉密尔顿**：是吗，泽基尔？

**泽基尔**：可不是嘛，老爷。

**汉密尔顿**：嗯，那又怎么了？

**泽基尔**：*（朝他走来）* 上校，您一整天几乎没沾过吃的，真要命，晚饭都候了这四个钟头啦。

**汉密尔顿**：是吗？那好，端到这儿来吧。

===Original===

**Zekiel**：Hi! Yo’ don’ eat at all then, sah. Yo’ jes’ looks at it an’ goes on wo’kin’.

**Hamilton**：*（Making correction on bill.）* I’m engaged on a difficult task. Uncle.

**Zekiel**：Yo’ shore mus’ be, Marse Ham’ton.

**Hamilton**：Trying to make bricks without straw.

**Zekiel**：Bricks! That cut’ny do seem a mighty pore sub-astute fer dinnah, Marse Cunnel.

**Hamilton**：I’m still out, Zekiel.

===Chinese===

**泽基尔**：嗨！那您也是白端，老爷。您只消瞧它一眼，接着又干活儿去了。

**汉密尔顿**：*（正在法案上作修改）* 我正忙着一件难差事呢，大叔。

**泽基尔**：那您准是够呛的，汉密尔顿老爷。

**汉密尔顿**：想做无米之炊呢。

**泽基尔**：砖头！那玩意儿拿来当晚饭，可实在寒碜得慌，上校老爷。

**汉密尔顿**：我还是「不在」，泽基尔。

===Original===

**Zekiel**：*（Turning at R. C.）* Reckon you-all bettah be home, Marse Cunnel, an’ quit wo’kin’ fo’ tonight.

**Hamilton**：I’m out, Zekiel, I’m out.

**Schuyler**：*（Without.）* What! Still out? I’ll come in, Uncle, and wait! *（Coming through hall door.）*

**Zekiel**：*（As **Schuyler** stumps in.）* So he’p me, Gen’al, ’tain’ no good yo’ waitin’.

**Hamilton**：*（Who has gotten up as soon as he heard voice.）* Why, father, I’ve been expecting you for hours.

**Schuyler**：Well, I called on you hours ago. *（To **Zekiel**.）* You black nigger, you, I don’t believe he’s been out at all!

===Chinese===

**泽基尔**：*（在台中偏右转过身）* 我看您哪，还是回家去的好，今晚就别干啦，上校老爷。

**汉密尔顿**：我「不在」，泽基尔，「不在」。

**斯凯勒**：*（幕后）* 什么！还「不在」？那我就进来等吧，大叔！*（穿过过道的门进来）*

**泽基尔**：*（斯凯勒拄拐进来时）* 老天爷作证，将军，您等也是白等。

**汉密尔顿**：*（一听见声音就起了身）* 哎呀，父亲，我等您好几个钟头了。

**斯凯勒**：嗯，几个钟头前我来拜访过你。*（对泽基尔）* 你这黑家伙，我看他压根儿就没「不在」过！

===Original===

**Zekiel**：*（Down R. of **Schuyler**.）* Why, he say he were out, and I done think he were.

**Hamilton**：*（L. of **Schuyler**.）* Uncle, I thought you knew I was expecting the General.

**Zekiel**：I don’ know nothin’ ’cept what you tells me, so help me, Marse Ham’ton.

**Hamilton**：I’m sorry, father, but you see as Secretary of the Treasury I’m a target for all kinds and conditions of people.

**Schuyler**：*（Sitting L. C.）* People who come to borrow money, eh?

**Hamilton**：*（Giving **Schuyler’s** hat to **Zekiel**.）* Exactly. That’s why I have to be “out, out, out.” You see there is still a large section of the public who regard the Treasury as a sort of savings-bank, from which they can withdraw money without the preliminary inconvenience of depositing it.

===Chinese===

**泽基尔**：*（走到斯凯勒右下方）* 咦，他说他不在嘛，我也就真当他不在。

**汉密尔顿**：*（在斯凯勒左侧）* 大叔，我还当您知道我正等着将军呢。

**泽基尔**：除了您吩咐的，我啥也不知道，老天爷作证，汉密尔顿老爷。

**汉密尔顿**：对不住，父亲。您也知道，我身为财政部长，三教九流的人都要冲着我来。

**斯凯勒**：*（在台中偏左坐下）* 都是来借钱的人，嗯？

**汉密尔顿**：*（把斯凯勒的帽子递给泽基尔）* 正是。所以我才不得不「不在，不在，不在」。您瞧，老百姓里头还有好大一拨人，把财政部当成个储蓄银行——不必先受存钱的麻烦，就能从里头取钱。

===Original===

**Schuyler**：*（Having risen to take off cloak, sits again.）* Well, the people are slow to understand. It’s only the last few years that we’ve had a Treasury.

**Hamilton**：*（C.）* Yes, we have a Treasury, but we haven’t any treasure. How’s the gout?

**Schuyler**：How do I find Colonel Hamilton? That’s what I want to know.

**Hamilton**：I’m perfectly well﻿—*（puts his hand across his eyes）* but I believe I’m tired. *（Crossing down L.）*

**Zekiel**：*（Advancing to R. C. from behind sofa.）* He ain’ had no victals sence mawnin’, Gen’l.

**Hamilton**：Ah, perhaps that’s it. I believe I’m hungry.

===Chinese===

**斯凯勒**：*（起身脱了外套，又坐下）* 唉，老百姓明白得慢。咱们有财政部，也不过才几年的事。

**汉密尔顿**：*（台中）* 是啊，财政部是有了，「财」可是一点儿没有。痛风怎么样了？

**斯凯勒**：汉密尔顿上校身子骨怎么样？我打听的是这个。

**汉密尔顿**：我好得很——*（抬手掩住眼睛）* 不过我觉着是累了。*（向左前方走）*

**泽基尔**：*（从沙发后头走到台中偏右）* 他打早上起就没沾过一口饭，将军。

**汉密尔顿**：啊，许是这么回事。我觉着是饿了。

===Original===

**Schuyler**：*（Turning angrily to **Zekiel**.）* Didn’t Mrs. Hamilton charge you before she went away to see that the Colonel ate his meals regularly?

**Zekiel**：Yes, sah, Mis’ Betsy she charge me, but ’fo’ Gawd, Marse Schuylah, I cain’ get him to eat scarcely a mouf’ful.

**Schuyler**：So that’s the way you look after your master, is it?

**Zekiel**：Sometimes, Gen’l, yo’ kin tak’ an’ lead a mule up to de troff﻿—*（**Hamilton**, lying up papers, protests, laughingly）* but eff he tak’ it into his haid not to drink﻿—wall﻿—yo’ cain’ do nothin’ ’bout it.

**Hamilton**：Uncle, you never said a word about drink. You’ve been warning me to eat all day. *（To **Schuyler**.）* What do you think I’ve been doing, father?

**Schuyler**：The Lord knows! Everybody’s work, as usual, I suppose.

===Chinese===

**斯凯勒**：*（恼火地转向泽基尔）* 汉密尔顿夫人临走之前，没嘱托你盯着上校按顿吃饭吗？

**泽基尔**：嘱托过，老爷，贝特西太太是嘱托了我。可老天在上，斯凯勒老爷，我连哄带劝，他一口也咽不下几口。

**斯凯勒**：你就是这么照应你家主人的，是吧？

**泽基尔**：有时候啊，将军，你就是牵着头骡子到槽边——*（汉密尔顿一面收拾文件，笑着抗议）* 可它要是打定了主意不喝——嗐——你就没辙了。

**汉密尔顿**：大叔，您可一个字没提过喝，您成天叮嘱的是吃。*（对斯凯勒）* 父亲，您猜我这一整天都在干什么？

**斯凯勒**：天知道！照例是替所有人干活儿呗，我猜。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：I’ve been building a National Bank.

**Schuyler**：Well, you can’t do it on an empty stomach.

**Hamilton**：Why not? I’m doing it on an empty Treasury.

**Schuyler**：*（To **Zekiel**, who is going toward door R.）* Uncle Zeke, you go and get up the best supper you know how, and I’ll see that the Colonel eats it. And a bottle of wine.

**Hamilton**：*（At escritoire up L.）* You’ll join me in that?

**Schuyler**：No, I’ve got a milk-fed foot.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：我一直在筹建一家国家银行。

**斯凯勒**：嗯，可你空着肚子干不成。

**汉密尔顿**：怎么干不成？我不就是靠着一空如洗的财政部在干嘛。

**斯凯勒**：*（对正往右侧门走的泽基尔）* 泽克大叔，去张罗一顿你顶拿手的晚饭，上校吃不吃，我来盯着。再添一瓶葡萄酒。

**汉密尔顿**：*（在左上写字台旁）* 那您陪我喝一杯？

**斯凯勒**：不成，我这只脚是吃奶喂大的。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：*（Laughing.）* Milk for General Schuyler.

**Zekiel**：Yassah. *（**Zekiel** exits L. happy.）*

**Hamilton**：*（Sitting on edge of table L. of **Schuyler**.）* Well, what news? You saw General Washington?

**Schuyler**：He’s in the lowest depths of depression, Alexander.

**Hamilton**：About the financial conditions.

**Schuyler**：He’s not as young as you, you know. He was born to fight﻿—but not to fight politicians.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：*（笑）* 给斯凯勒将军上牛奶。

**泽基尔**：好嘞，老爷。*（泽基尔高高兴兴自左下）*

**汉密尔顿**：*（在斯凯勒左边的桌沿上坐下）* 好了，有什么新闻？您见到华盛顿将军了？

**斯凯勒**：他消沉到了底儿，亚历山大。

**汉密尔顿**：为着财政的景况。

**斯凯勒**：他可不比你年轻，要知道。他生来是打仗的——不是跟政客打仗的。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：Well, I’m going to do the fighting now. *（Picks up pens.）* Here’s a whole new bundle of pens and I’m going to stick a man with every one of them.

**Schuyler**：The opposition have half a dozen bundles to your one, and they poison the points.

**Hamilton**：That’s just it. They use too many pens and so the poison fails to take effect. I’ve got them sticking all over me, and I can’t even feel them.

**Schuyler**：But Washington feels them. They’re always attacking him. The latest is an accusation that he is drawing more salary than he is entitled to!

**Hamilton**：Whose work is that, Tom Paine’s?

**Schuyler**：No, the clerk of the house. But Tom Paine has written him a letter, too, accusing him of incompetence, calling him “Treacherous in private friendship, a hypocrite in public life.”

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：好吧，这一仗如今换我来打。*（拿起笔）* 这儿是崭新一捆笔，我要拿它们一支一支地扎，一支扎倒一个人。

**斯凯勒**：对面一捆顶你六捆，笔尖上还淬着毒。

**汉密尔顿**：病根儿就在这儿。他们的笔使得太多，毒性反倒不灵了。我满身都扎着他们的笔，可连觉都觉不出来。

**斯凯勒**：可华盛顿觉着疼。他们总在攻讦他。最新一条罪状，说他领的薪水超过了应得之数！

**汉密尔顿**：那是谁的手笔，汤姆·潘恩的吗？

**斯凯勒**：不是，是众议院的一个文书。不过汤姆·潘恩也给他写了封信，指斥他无能，骂他「私交上背信弃义，公职中虚伪作假」。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：Sounds like Tom Paine.

**Schuyler**：And heaven knows what besides.

**Hamilton**：Ungrateful scoundrels!

**Schuyler**：The thing that hits him hardest is their everlasting hooting about the army. George Washington loves his army as he would have loved an only child.

**Hamilton**：He has beggared himself in an attempt to meet the country’s promise to pay. It’s the old story. The greater the achievement of the man, the more violent his detractors. *（Enter **Zekiel**, L., with tray containing chicken, bread and butter, jug of milk, glass, bottle of wine, wineglass, napkin and white cloth on tray.）* Now they are shouting “Dictator” and accusing him of trying to make himself king. And they know it’s a lie.

**Schuyler**：Of course it’s a lie﻿—that’s why the politicians glory in it.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：一听就是汤姆·潘恩的腔调。

**斯凯勒**：还有天知道多少别的名堂。

**汉密尔顿**：忘恩负义的恶棍！

**斯凯勒**：最叫他痛心的，是他们没完没了地冲着军队叫嚣。乔治·华盛顿爱他那支军队，就像爱独生的骨肉。

**汉密尔顿**：他为了兑现国家还债的承诺，把自己弄得一贫如洗。老故事了：一个人的功业越大，诋毁他的人闹得越凶。*（泽基尔自左上，端着托盘，盘中有鸡肉、黄油面包、一罐牛奶、一只玻璃杯、一瓶葡萄酒、一只酒杯、餐巾，还有铺盘的白布。）* 如今他们又嚷嚷「独裁者」，指斥他想自立为王。而他们清楚这是谎话。

**斯凯勒**：自然是谎话——正因是谎话，政客们才拿它当光彩。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：You can’t lead the people with a lie. The truth.

**Schuyler**：Uncle Zeke, I hope you hear your master talking about the value of telling the truth. It will do you good.

**Zekiel**：*（Having placed tray on table C.）* ‘Fo’ Gawd, Marse Gen’l, the only lie I evah tell is to say Marse Ham’ton’s out when he’s in﻿—an’ shorely that’s a mighty white lie.

**Schuyler**：For a gentleman of your color.

**Hamilton**：Well, Zekiel, if anyone calls, I’m in for tonight.

**Schuyler**：Unless they want money out of the Treasury.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：谎话领不动人民。要真相。

**斯凯勒**：泽克大叔，但愿你听见你家主人讲诚实的可贵了。对你有好处。

**泽基尔**：*（已把托盘放到中桌上）* 老天在上，将军老爷，我这辈子唯一扯过的谎，就是说汉密尔顿老爷「不在」其实他在家——那总归算个顶「白」的谎吧。

**斯凯勒**：就凭您这肤色的绅士而言。

**汉密尔顿**：好了，泽基尔，今晚再有人来，就说我在家。

**斯凯勒**：除非他们是想从财政部里弄钱。

===Original===

**Zekiel**：*（Taking chair from L. of bookcase at back and placing it back of table C.）* Very well, Marse Hamilton, yo’s in fo’ tonight. Now yo’ eat that thar dinnah, an’ I’ll bring yo’ mo’ to follow.

**Schuyler**：Now! What have we here? Chicken.

**Hamilton**：*（At escritoire.）* Ah!

**Schuyler**：And bread and butter. Damn it, there’s no pie!

**Hamilton**：*（Coming over.）* Good heavens! No pie!

**Schuyler**：*（Calling.）* Zekiel!

===Chinese===

**泽基尔**：*（把书柜左边的一把椅子搬来放在中桌后）* 好嘞，汉密尔顿老爷，今晚您在家。这会儿您把那顿饭吃了，回头我再给您添。

**斯凯勒**：哟！这儿都是什么呀？鸡肉。

**汉密尔顿**：*（在写字台旁）* 啊！

**斯凯勒**：还有黄油面包。真见鬼，没有馅饼！

**汉密尔顿**：*（走过来）* 天哪！没有馅饼！

**斯凯勒**：*（扬声喊）* 泽基尔！

===Original===

**Hamilton**：I’ll wager he’s gone to get the pie.

**Schuyler**：Well, it doesn’t seem much to go building banks on.

**Hamilton**：How can I build banks without pie!

**Schuyler**：It’s time Betsy came back. Thank God, she will be home next week.

**Hamilton**：*（Standing back of table C.）* Haven’t you had a letter by the last packet? *（Knock ready.）*

**Schuyler**：No. Why, I’ve been on the road from Albany the last five days. What’s wrong?

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：我敢打赌，他准是取馅饼去了。

**斯凯勒**：嗐，就凭这个，可不像能建起银行的样子。

**汉密尔顿**：没有馅饼，我怎么建银行！

**斯凯勒**：贝特西也该回来了。谢天谢地，她下星期就到家。

**汉密尔顿**：*（站到中桌后）* 上一班邮船没给您捎信来？*（正待有人敲门）*

**斯凯勒**：没有。嗐，我从奥尔巴尼一路赶来，这都走了五天了。出什么事了？

===Original===

**Hamilton**：*（Taking out letter from his breast pocket.）* Nothing wrong. But Betsy writes to say that Angelica isn’t able to come as soon as they expected. It will be another month before they are here.

**Schuyler**：It’s a damned shame the way you’ve been left with no one to look after you. Go on and eat. Betsy had no sort of business to go off and leave you at all. I say it if she is my own daughter.

**Hamilton**：Why, you begged and prayed of her to go.

**Schuyler**：Well, she ought to be back.

**Hamilton**：She’s coming back. Heaven knows, I miss Betsy.

**Schuyler**：*（Fuming.）* President Washington leans on you like a child on its mother, and not a soul in this whole town sees to it that you have any﻿—any﻿—

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：*（从胸前口袋取出信）* 没出事。只是贝特西信上说，安杰莉卡不便照她们原先指望的早些动身，她们还得再过一个月才能到。

**斯凯勒**：撂下你一个人没人照应，真是岂有此理。快去吃饭。贝特西压根儿就不该一走了之。哪怕她是我亲闺女，我也这么说。

**汉密尔顿**：咦，当初还是您求了又求，劝她去的呢。

**斯凯勒**：嗐，她也该回来了。

**汉密尔顿**：她会回来的。天知道，我想念贝特西。

**斯凯勒**：*（气不打一处来）* 华盛顿总统倚着你，就像孩子倚着娘。可这满城的人，没一个操心你有没有——有没有——

===Original===

**Hamilton**：Any pie!

**Schuyler**：Eat, I say﻿—eat! *（Knock.）* Now, there’s somebody else. Hope that nigger has sense enough to say you’re out.

**Hamilton**：*（Opening napkin.）* Come on then, join me. I’m hungry as a hunter. *（Passes milk.）* There’s your milk.

**Schuyler**：Milk! Bah! *（Enter **Zekiel**.）*

**Zekiel**：*（At door R., closing it behind him. Woman seen in hall as he enters.）* Here’s a poor woman at the door with a baby in her arms. She wants to see you, sah. Is yo’ in or is yo’ out?

**Schuyler**：Out!

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：馅饼！

**斯凯勒**：吃，我说——快吃！*（敲门声）* 瞧，又来人了。但愿那黑家伙脑子够用，就说你不在。

**汉密尔顿**：*（抖开餐巾）* 来吧，陪我一块儿吃。我饿得跟猎人似的。*（递过牛奶）* 您的牛奶。

**斯凯勒**：牛奶！呸！*（泽基尔上）*

**泽基尔**：*（在右侧门口，随手带上门。他进来时门厅里可见一名妇人）* 门口来了个穷苦女人，怀里抱着个娃娃。她要见您，老爷。您是在家还是不在？

**斯凯勒**：不在！

===Original===

**Hamilton**：What does she want?

**Zekiel**：She says as how she’s de wife ob one ob de soldiers.

**Hamilton**：*（To **Schuyler**.）* You see﻿—she comes to me for money﻿—money out of the Treasury! I’ll see her, Uncle. *（**Schuyler** objects, but **Zekiel** opens door.）* Can’t send her away, father.

**Schuyler**：*（Mutters.）* You can’t live without food. What’s the use﻿—

**Hamilton**：What can I do for you?

**Woman**：*（Coming to R. C.）* Are you Colonel Hamilton?

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：她有什么事？

**泽基尔**：她说呀，她是一个大兵的媳妇。

**汉密尔顿**：*（对斯凯勒）* 您瞧——她冲我来是为钱——财政部里的钱！我去见她，大叔。*（斯凯勒不赞成，泽基尔还是开了门）* 总不能把她打发走，父亲。

**斯凯勒**：*（咕哝）* 你不吃东西也活不成。有什么用——

**汉密尔顿**：我能为您做点什么？

**妇人**：*（走到台中偏右）* 您是汉密尔顿上校吗？

===Original===

**Hamilton**：Yes. *（Pulling the chair he was sitting on.）* Sit down. *（She sits.）* What is it?

**Woman**：My husband is Zackery Whalen, sir. He fought in the war, sir. He’s a cripple and can’t work. He fought under you, sir. It was that winter at Valley Forge﻿—his feet froze so many times, sir. He’s on a pension, but we can’t get the money.

**Hamilton**：Can’t get it from your state?

**Woman**：We sometimes get it, but not lately at all﻿—only promises, sir. You see, ours is due from Rhode Island. If it had been Virginia or New Hampshire, it’d been all right, because they are paying their men, but Rhode Island says they can’t.

**Hamilton**：What do you wish me to do?

**Woman**：I heard General Washington spoke at a meeting of the soldiers last night and he gave his word that everyone would be paid. I know he will keep his word, sir, but we can’t wait.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：是。*（把自己方才坐的椅子拉过来）* 请坐。*（她坐下）* 什么事？

**妇人**：我丈夫是扎克里·惠伦，先生。他打过仗，先生。他成了残废，干不了活了。他是在您麾下打的仗，先生。就是福吉谷那个冬天——他那双脚冻坏了一回又一回，先生。他领着抚恤，可那钱我们拿不到手。

**汉密尔顿**：州里不发给你们？

**妇人**：往常还能领着，可近来压根儿领不到了——只剩空口许诺，先生。您瞧，我们这份饷是罗得岛欠下的。要是弗吉尼亚或是新罕布什尔倒还好，它们给自己的士兵发饷，可罗得岛说发不出。

**汉密尔顿**：您要我怎么办呢？

**妇人**：我听说华盛顿将军昨晚在士兵集会上讲了话，担保每个人都会拿到饷。我知道他说到做到，先生，可我们等不起啊。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：*（Kindly.）* Why did you come to me?

**Woman**：They told me that you were Secretary of the Treasury, where all the money is.

**Hamilton**：*（Giving another hopeless look at **Schuyler**, who grunts.）* I wish I could make you understand. The Treasury at present is only a name﻿—an empty name. *（Takes paper from **Woman.**）* This is Rhode Island’s promise to pay. Mrs. Whalen, I am trying to make the country keep this promise; I’m trying to make them pay.

**Woman**：How long shall we have to wait, sir?

**Hamilton**：I don’t know. Here *（trying two waistcoat pockets before finding it）* take this.

**Woman**：Thank you, sir.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：*（和气地）* 您怎么找到我这儿来了？

**妇人**：人家跟我说，您是财政部长，钱都在您那儿。

**汉密尔顿**：*（又朝斯凯勒投去无可奈何的一瞥，斯凯勒哼了一声）* 但愿我能叫您明白。如今的财政部只是个名号——一个空名号。*（从妇人手里接过那张纸）* 这就是罗得岛的还债承诺。惠伦太太，我正设法叫这个国家兑现这份承诺；我正设法叫他们还钱。

**妇人**：我们还得等多久，先生？

**汉密尔顿**：我说不上。这儿——*（摸了两回马甲口袋才找着）* 拿着这个。

**妇人**：谢谢您，先生。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：*（Following her a step or two.）* I can only say that I’ll do my best to see that you are paid. You’re hungry, aren’t you?

**Woman**：Yes, sir.

**Hamilton**：*（Takes four corners of napkin and ties chicken inside and hands to her.）* Here, take this chicken and have a jolly good supper with your husband. I’m afraid there isn’t much for the﻿—*（He leans over and tenderly removes a corner of the shawl from the head of the sleeping baby.）* What’s the baby’s name?

**Woman**：Elizabeth, sir.

**Hamilton**：*（Smiling.）* Elizabeth﻿—is it?

**Woman**：Yes, sir.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：*（跟着她走了一两步）* 我只能说，我必定竭尽所能，叫你们拿到该得的钱。您饿了，是不是？

**妇人**：是的，先生。

**汉密尔顿**：*（拎起餐巾四角，把鸡肉包在里头递给她）* 来，把这只鸡带上，跟您丈夫好好吃一顿晚饭。只怕这点东西不够——*（他俯下身，轻轻掀开熟睡婴儿头上的披巾一角）* 孩子叫什么名字？

**妇人**：伊丽莎白，先生。

**汉密尔顿**：*（微笑）* 伊丽莎白——是吗？

**妇人**：是的，先生。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：*（Taking jug of milk from **Schuyler**, who was to pour some out.）* The baby’s name is Betsy, father. *（Gives jug to **Woman**.）* Milk for Elizabeth! *（**Zekiel** and **Schuyler** very protesting.）* Zekiel, show Mrs. Whalen out. Tell your husband I’m fighting for the men who fought for me, and it’s a harder struggle than we had at Valley Forge. But that I mean to win, as we won at Yorktown.

**Woman**：*（Near room-door.）* Thank you, sir. I’m sorry to have troubled you, but it’s hard on the women. When the war broke out, we had to let our men go and proud we were of ’em﻿—and when my husband came back disabled and useless, everybody took him by the hand and helped him. That was when the war was on. But now it’s finished.﻿ ﻿… It’s hard we can’t get paid. Good night, sir.

**Hamilton**：*（Turning to **Schuyler** with a groan.）* The disgrace of it! The men who won our freedom left to starve!

**Schuyler**：If Tom Jefferson and those damned Anti-Federalists would let your Assumption Bill go through, why, the soldiers would be paid.

**Hamilton**：*（Walking excitedly across to L.）* Their opposition to this bill is holding the country in the grip of bankruptcy.

**Schuyler**：While Jefferson and Monroe oppose it you’ll never get it through.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：*（从斯凯勒手里接过奶罐——他本要倒些出来）* 父亲，这娃娃的名字正是贝特西。*（把奶罐递给妇人）* 给伊丽莎白的牛奶！*（泽基尔和斯凯勒连连抗议）* 泽基尔，送惠伦太太出去。告诉您丈夫，我正为那些曾为我战斗的人而战斗，这一仗比我们在福吉谷那阵还难打。但我决意打赢，就像我们在约克镇打赢的那样。

**妇人**：*（在房门近旁）* 谢谢您，先生。打搅您了，真对不住，可我们做女人的日子难熬。打起仗那会儿，我们只得放男人走，还为他们自豪——我丈夫回来时残了废、不中用了，人人都拉他的手、帮衬他。那是仗还打着的时候。如今仗打完了。……偏偏该我们的钱领不着了，真叫人寒心。晚安，先生。

**汉密尔顿**：*（呻吟一声，转向斯凯勒）* 这是耻辱啊！为我们赢得自由的人，竟落到挨饿的地步！

**斯凯勒**：要是汤姆·杰斐逊和那帮该死的反联邦党人肯放你的州债承担法案过关，大兵们早就拿到饷了。

**汉密尔顿**：*（激动地走到左边）* 他们死卡着这项法案，等于把整个国家攥在破产的手心里。

**斯凯勒**：只要杰斐逊和门罗一天反对，你就一天通不过它。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：*（Back of his table, picking up manuscript.）* I’ll fight for it﻿—I’ll fight for it to the end. *（Throwing down manuscript and crossing at back to R.）* You see, father, Jefferson never smelt the smoke of battle.

**Schuyler**：No. He prides himself on being a man of peace. All he and his flock do is to go around shouting “States’ Rights.”

**Hamilton**：Thomas Jefferson and the Rights of Man! He gets his followers to do the shouting while he writes for posterity. *（Placing his chair R. of small table.）*

**Schuyler**：*（Grudgingly.）* Yes, he writes well.

**Hamilton**：*（Standing R. C.）* He writes music﻿—the music of well-chosen words.

**Schuyler**：And the people listen to him.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：*（走到桌后，拾起手稿）* 我要为它战斗——为它战斗到底。*（撂下手稿，从台后走到右边）* 您瞧，父亲，杰斐逊可从来没闻过战场的硝烟。

**斯凯勒**：可不是。他还以和平人士自居呢。他和他那一群人干的，不过是满处嚷嚷「州权」罢了。

**汉密尔顿**：托马斯·杰斐逊和人权！他叫追随者去喊，自己只管写给后世看。*（把椅子放到小桌右边）*

**斯凯勒**：*（不大情愿地）* 是啊，他文章写得好。

**汉密尔顿**：*（站在台中偏右）* 他写的是音乐——字斟句酌的音乐。

**斯凯勒**：老百姓也听得进去。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：We all listen to Tom Jefferson’s music. He’s like the Pied Piper. He pipes and he pipes, and the people follow spellbound.

**Schuyler**：He can certainly pipe.

**Hamilton**：Of course there is always the danger that he will lead them into the sea.

**Schuyler**：Well, there’s a lot of rats running after him that’d be all the better for drowning.

**Hamilton**：There are always rats running up the backstairs trying to nibble their way into office.

**Schuyler**：Wouldn’t be if the offices were kept clean.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：谁都听得进汤姆·杰斐逊的曲子。他就像花衣魔笛手，吹啊，吹啊，人们便着了魔似的跟着走。

**斯凯勒**：他那笛子，吹得也真是一绝。

**汉密尔顿**：当然喽，这笛声迟早有把他们引进海里的危险。

**斯凯勒**：嗐，跟在他后头跑的老鼠多着呢，淹死一批倒干净。

**汉密尔顿**：总有老鼠顺着后楼梯溜上来，想啃出一条钻进官位的路。

**斯凯勒**：官署要是保持得干净，就没有了。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：Only a strong government can keep the offices clean. This policy of every man for himself is leading the country to anarchy. *（He is very angry.）*

**Zekiel**：*（A little alarmed.）* Reckon I bettah get yo’ some mo’ suppah, Cunnel.

**Schuyler**：Don’t you get me any more of that damned milk.

**Zekiel**：Mebbe I’d jes’ bettah fetch a little in a feedin’ bottle, Gen’l, yah, yah!

**Schuyler**：When Zekiel lied to me this morning and said you were out, I went round to the Exchange Coffee House.

**Hamilton**：To get a milk punch?

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：只有一个强有力的政府，才保得住官署干净。这种人人为己的做派，正把国家引向无政府状态。*（他怒不可遏）*

**泽基尔**：*（有点发慌）* 我看我还是给您再添点儿晚饭吧，上校。

**斯凯勒**：你可别再给我上那该死的牛奶了。

**泽基尔**：兴许我还是拿奶瓶给您装一点儿来好咧，将军，嘿嘿！

**斯凯勒**：今儿早上泽基尔骗我说你不在，我就遛到交易咖啡馆去了。

**汉密尔顿**：去喝牛奶潘趣？

===Original===

**Schuyler**：To look for you! I saw several of the rats there, and I’m convinced they’re hatching something for you.

**Hamilton**：I always associate hatching with chickens, but I dare say rats do it. Well?

**Schuyler**：Be on your guard. Jefferson and Monroe are coming to see you about the location of the Capital.

**Hamilton**：*（Unconcerned.）* Oh, the Residence Bill! *（Takes out handkerchief.）* Well, what about it?

**Schuyler**：*（Sitting back; nettled.）* What about it!

**Hamilton**：I mean where do they want the Capital? In Jefferson’s parlor, I suppose.

===Chinese===

**斯凯勒**：去找你！我在那儿瞧见了好几只老鼠，我敢说，他们正给你孵着什么坏主意呢。

**汉密尔顿**：我一向觉得孵蛋是母鸡的事，不过老鼠兴许也会。怎么说？

**斯凯勒**：你得提防着点儿。杰斐逊和门罗要来见你，谈首都选址的事。

**汉密尔顿**：*（满不在乎）* 哦，定居法案！*（掏出手帕）* 那又怎么样？

**斯凯勒**：*（往椅背上一靠，动了气）* 还「怎么样」！

**汉密尔顿**：我是说，他们想把首都定在哪儿？我猜，是定在杰斐逊家的客厅里。

===Original===

**Schuyler**：That’s just where they do want it. They want the Capital of the United States in the South. My God!

**Hamilton**：But﻿—excuse me.

**Schuyler**：*（Fuming.）* My God!

**Hamilton**：Is that the end of your prayer, or the beginning, father?

**Schuyler**：The South!

**Hamilton**：Well, why not?

===Chinese===

**斯凯勒**：他们还正就想定在那儿。他们要把合众国的首都弄到南方去。我的天！

**汉密尔顿**：可是——失陪一下。

**斯凯勒**：*（气得直喘）* 我的天！

**汉密尔顿**：父亲，您这祷词是念到头了，还是刚起头？

**斯凯勒**：南方！

**汉密尔顿**：嗯，为什么不能是南方？

===Original===

**Schuyler**：Do you mean to say you’d let the Capital of the United States go to the *South?*

**Hamilton**：Where do you think it should be?

**Schuyler**：Where should it be? Why Albany, of course!

**Hamilton**：*（Jumping out of his chair and over to R., laughing heartily.）* Oh, your home town.

**Schuyler**：The finest city on God’s earth!

**Hamilton**：*（Laughs.）* In *your* parlor, I suppose. No, General. Certainly not Albany. *（Sits in chair again.）*

===Chinese===

**斯凯勒**：你的意思是，你竟肯让合众国的首都落到*南方*去？

**汉密尔顿**：那您觉得该定在哪儿？

**斯凯勒**：该定在哪儿？嗐，当然是奥尔巴尼！

**汉密尔顿**：*（从椅子里跳起来，蹦到右边，放声大笑）* 哦，您的老家。

**斯凯勒**：上帝脚下顶好的城市！

**汉密尔顿**：*（笑）* 我猜，就定在您家客厅里。不，将军。奥尔巴尼绝对不行。*（重新坐下）*

===Original===

**Schuyler**：*（Nettled.）* Oh, certainly not Albany, eh? And why certainly not Albany? I suppose you want it in New York?

**Hamilton**：New York? Nnn-o﻿—

**Schuyler**：Well, in heaven’s name, where do you want it?

**Hamilton**：Anywhere that’s handy to get at.

**Schuyler**：Well, I’ll be﻿—! Haven’t you any patriotism, man?

**Hamilton**：I don’t care where the Capital is, or whether it’s built of marble or whether it’s made of wood, so long as we get the right men inside﻿—to restore law and order to this limping, half-starved government.

===Chinese===

**斯凯勒**：*（被激恼）* 哦，奥尔巴尼绝对不行，嗯？为什么绝对不行？我猜你想定在纽约？

**汉密尔顿**：纽约？唔——不——

**斯凯勒**：那么老天在上，你到底想定在哪儿？

**汉密尔顿**：哪儿进出方便就定哪儿。

**斯凯勒**：嗐，你可真——！你这个人，还有没有一点儿爱国心？

**汉密尔顿**：首都定在哪儿我不在乎，是大理石砌的还是木头搭的也不在乎，只要里头坐的是合适的人——好替这个瘸着腿、半饥半饱的政府恢复法律与秩序。

===Original===

**Schuyler**：Alexander﻿—

**Hamilton**：*（Puling his chair around to face **Schuyler** and anticipating amusement.）* What *are* the reasons why the Capital should be in the North?

**Schuyler**：Why! All the traditions connected with our struggle for independence cluster about the North.

**Hamilton**：*（Ticking it off on his fingers.）* There’s some truth in that.

**Schuyler**：It was here in Philadelphia that the Declaration of Independence was signed.

**Hamilton**：*（Checking on fingers.）* So it was.

===Chinese===

**斯凯勒**：亚历山大——

**汉密尔顿**：*（把椅子转过来正对斯凯勒，先就料到有好戏看）* 首都凭什么都该定在北方呢？

**斯凯勒**：还用问！咱们独立大业的全部传统，都聚在北方。

**汉密尔顿**：*（扳着指头记数）* 这话有几分道理。

**斯凯勒**：《独立宣言》就是在这儿、在费城签署的。

**汉密尔顿**：*（扳指记下）* 确是如此。

===Original===

**Schuyler**：It was in New York that Washington took the oath of office. *（**Hamilton** checks.）* In New York the government had its beginning. Why, Albany commands the commerce of the four corners of the earth.

**Hamilton**：*（Checking.）* Hummm!

**Schuyler**：And﻿—and there’s not a damned bit of reason why the South should have it anyway.

**Hamilton**：But if the North has all the glory of the traditions of the past, isn’t it a good reason why the South should be glorified with the hopes of the future?

**Schuyler**：No!﻿ ﻿… *（**Hamilton** rises.）*

**Hamilton**：Hasn’t the South, at any rate, as much claim as the North?

===Chinese===

**斯凯勒**：华盛顿就是在纽约宣誓就职的。*（汉密尔顿扳指记下）* 政府就是在纽约开的端。再者，奥尔巴尼扼着天下四方的商路。

**汉密尔顿**：*（又扳一指）* 嗯哼！

**斯凯勒**：还有——还有，凭什么该让南方得着，连半点儿道理都没有。

**汉密尔顿**：可北方既然占全了往昔传统的荣光，把未来希望的荣光分给南方，不也正是桩美事？

**斯凯勒**：不行！……*（汉密尔顿起身）*

**汉密尔顿**：退一万步说，南方的资格难道不跟北方一样？

===Original===

**Schuyler**：No!﻿ ﻿… *（Enter **Zekiel**, R.）*

**Hamilton**：General, your argument is﻿—

**Zekiel**：Secatary Jeff’son and Sentah Monroe callin’ to see yo’, sir. Reckon you’s out?

**Hamilton**：I reckon I’m in, Zekiel.

**Schuyler**：*（Rising.）* Now, here they come to talk to you about the Capital, and you’re going to concede it without a struggle.

**Hamilton**：*（Coming to him.）* Father, you’re the best friend I have in the world, but I daren’t trust you in a matter of diplomacy. That’s no reflection on your intelligence because, you know, diplomacy is frequently only one-eighth brain *（tapping him on the forehead）* and seven-eighths the way you use it. I’m going to send you out onto that balcony to cool down. *（Going up toward C. window.）*

===Chinese===

**斯凯勒**：不行！……*（泽基尔自右上）*

**汉密尔顿**：将军，您这套论证——

**泽基尔**：杰斐逊部长大人和门罗参议员来拜见您了，老爷。我看您是「不在」吧？

**汉密尔顿**：我看我在家，泽基尔。

**斯凯勒**：*（起身）* 瞧，他们来跟你谈首都的事了，你倒好，一仗不打就要拱手让出去。

**汉密尔顿**：*（走到他跟前）* 父亲，您是我在世上顶好的朋友，可在外交这档子事上，我不敢信托您。这可不是小看您的智谋——要知道，外交这东西，常常八分之一靠脑子，*（点点他的额头）* 八分之七靠怎么使唤这个脑子。我要请您上阳台凉快凉快去。*（向中间的窗走去）*

===Original===

**Zekiel**：I jes’ got some mo’ suppah ready to brung up.

**Schuyler**：*（Getting above centre table.）* Well, bring it in, Zekiel, and the country be hanged.

**Hamilton**：*（Coming down R. of **Schuyler** and taking his arm.）* Father, I wonder if they feel as strongly as you do about the location of the Capital.

**Schuyler**：*（Raising his hand to strike the small table and breaking out.）* Why, any man with an ounce of﻿—

**Hamilton**：Out in the cool air, father. *（Pushes him on to the balcony.）* Oh, General, I’ve noticed that there sometimes comes a moment in diplomatic conferences when a little diversion is most valuable. Should I call for you, come in *（**Schuyler** grunts）*﻿—and look pleasant. I’ll see them, Zekiel. *（**Zekiel** exits R. **Hamilton** goes to his table and picking up manuscript of his bill returns to window and calls.）* Father, if I could only get hold of Jefferson and Monroe!

**Schuyler**：To back your Assumption Bill?

===Chinese===

**泽基尔**：我刚又备好一份晚饭，正要端上来呢。

**斯凯勒**：*（走到中桌上方）* 那就端进来吧，泽基尔，国家去它的。

**汉密尔顿**：*（走到斯凯勒右边，挽起他的胳膊）* 父亲，也不知他们两位对首都选址，是不是跟您一样上心。

**斯凯勒**：*（抬手要拍小桌，发作起来）* 嗐，但凡一个人有一星半点儿——

**汉密尔顿**：到外头凉快去，父亲。*（把他推上阳台）* 哦，将军，我发现外交会谈里有时会碰上这样的关口：来点儿小小的岔开最是金贵。我要是唤您，您就进来*（斯凯勒哼了一声）*——还得面带和气。我去见他们，泽基尔。*（泽基尔自右下。汉密尔顿走到桌前，拿起他法案的手稿，回到窗边唤道）* 父亲，我要是能把杰斐逊和门罗攥在手心里就好了！

**斯凯勒**：好给你那州债承担法案撑腰？

===Original===

**Hamilton**：Yes.

**Schuyler**：If you could only get hold of the moon!

**Zekiel**：Sect’y Jeff’son and Sentah Monroe.

**Hamilton**：*（Bowing to them.）* Mr. Jefferson. Senator Monroe.

**Monroe**：*（L. of **Jefferson**.）* Howdo, Hamilton.

**Jefferson**：*（Advancing to C.）* Citizen Secretary, I trust we do not call at an inconvenient or unseasonable hour.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：正是。

**斯凯勒**：你怎么不上天把月亮攥下来！

**泽基尔**：杰斐逊部长大人和门罗参议员到。

**汉密尔顿**：*（向二人欠身）* 杰斐逊先生。门罗参议员。

**门罗**：*（在杰斐逊左侧）* 你好哇，汉密尔顿。

**杰斐逊**：*（走到台中）* 部长公民，但愿我们来得不算不便，也不失时宜。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：Your time, gentlemen, could not have been better chosen. Zekiel, a bottle of wine.

**Zekiel**：A bottle of wine and a sangwidge. Yes, sah.

**Jefferson**：Mrs. Hamilton is not back yet?

**Hamilton**：*（Getting behind chair R. of large table and indicating it to **Jefferson**.）* No, my wife will not return, I fear, for a month or more. Will you take this chair, Mr. Jefferson? *（**Jefferson** sits R. of table.）* Will you take this one, Senator? *（**Monroe** crosses and sits in large armchair.）* That is the chair General Washington always sits in when he is here. *（**Monroe** rises uncomfortably then sits again.）* I’lll sit here at my desk, if you don’t mind.

**Jefferson**：You are still working even at this late hour, Citizen Hamilton?

**Hamilton**：Oh, no. This is my recreation.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：二位先生，这个时辰挑得再好不过了。泽基尔，来一瓶葡萄酒。

**泽基尔**：一瓶葡萄酒，再搁块三明治。好嘞，老爷。

**杰斐逊**：汉密尔顿夫人还没回来？

**汉密尔顿**：*（走到大桌右侧椅子后，指给杰斐逊）* 没有，我恐怕一两个月内回不来。您坐这把椅子吧，杰斐逊先生？*（杰斐逊在桌右落座）* 您坐这把，参议员？*（门罗走过去坐进大扶手椅）* 华盛顿将军来的时候，坐的总是这把椅子。*（门罗如坐针毡，起身，又坐下）* 二位不介意的话，我就坐在我这书桌旁。

**杰斐逊**：这么晚的时辰，您还在办公，汉密尔顿公民？

**汉密尔顿**：哦，不。这是我的消遣。

===Original===

**Monroe**：And what is your recreation?

**Hamilton**：The establishment of a National Bank.

**Monroe**：It’s not constitutional.

**Hamilton**：It will be necessary for the full development of my Bill for the Government Assumption of States’ Debts.

**Jefferson**：A very charming room you have here.

**Hamilton**：Very charming.

===Chinese===

**门罗**：那您的消遣是什么？

**汉密尔顿**：筹建一家国家银行。

**门罗**：那可不合宪法。

**汉密尔顿**：要让我那项政府承担各州债务的州债法案充分施展，它必不可少。

**杰斐逊**：您这间屋子真是雅致得很。

**汉密尔顿**：雅致得很。

===Original===

**Monroe**：Devilish hot, though.

**Hamilton**：Allow me to open this window. *（Going to balcony window.）* I should mention that General Schuyler is on the balcony, if our business is private.

**Jefferson**：No, no, not in the least. Pray consider our visit as quite informal. *（**Hamilton** opens window.）*

**Monroe**：We came to have a little *friendly* chat with you.

**Hamilton**：*（Returning to table.）* Gentlemen, I am indeed relieved to find that you have come in this friendly spirit. It gives me courage to approach you in a perfectly friendly way on a very urgent matter.

**Jefferson**：I trust it may be within our power to be of use to you.

===Chinese===

**门罗**：就是热得邪乎。

**汉密尔顿**：容我把这扇窗打开。*（走向阳台的窗）* 我该说一句，斯凯勒将军正在阳台上——要是我们谈的事需要背人的话。

**杰斐逊**：不，不，丝毫不必。请只当我们是寻常走动。*（汉密尔顿开窗）*

**门罗**：我们来，是想同您小小的*友好*一叙。

**汉密尔顿**：*（回到桌边）* 二位先生，见你们带着这份友好而来，我着实宽慰。这也给了我胆量，以全然友好的方式，向二位提一件万分紧急的事。

**杰斐逊**：但愿在我们力所能及的范围之内。

===Original===

**Monroe**：Without sacrificing the principles for which we stand.

**Jefferson**：Of course.

**Hamilton**：*（Sitting back of table.）* President Washington is deeply concerned at the country’s neglect to pay its debts﻿—the debts incurred during the war.

**Monroe**：You mean the neglect of certain states to pay their debts?

**Hamilton**：I mean the neglect of the nation.

**Monroe**：Virginia has paid every cent she owes.

===Chinese===

**门罗**：前提是不牺牲我们秉持的原则。

**杰斐逊**：那自然。

**汉密尔顿**：*（在桌后坐下）* 华盛顿总统深为忧虑：这个国家迟迟不偿还它的债务——战争期间欠下的债务。

**门罗**：您是说某些州迟迟不还债？

**汉密尔顿**：我说的是这个国家在懈怠。

**门罗**：弗吉尼亚欠下的账，一分一厘都付清了。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：That saves the honor of Virginia, but not the honor of the nation. Gentlemen, my Bill provides that these debts shall be assumed by the central government. Why do you continue to oppose it? All I need is the support of Thomas Jefferson and James Monroe.

**Jefferson**：What you suggest would be making Virginia help to pay the debt of South Carolina.

**Monroe**：And South Carolina has three times the debt of Virginia.

**Hamilton**：*（To **Monroe**.）* Incurred in gaining Virginia her freedom. Gentlemen, if the interests of the thirteen states continue to clash, there is no hope of established independence. I appeal to you both, as patriots, not to squander the time of the country by the discussion of party interests. We are builders of a nation; let us build strongly, let us build on the foundation stone of honor. The nations of the world are watching us. Let them sneer at our youth, let them sneer at our poverty, but let them never cast a slur upon our honesty.

**Jefferson**：You plead merely for the honor of the nation: I plead also for the rights of the individual.

**Monroe**：Do you realize that the rights of the people are at stake?

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：那保住的是弗吉尼亚的体面，保不住这个国家的体面。二位先生，我的法案明定：这些债务由中央政府承担。二位为何还要一味反对？我需要的，无非是托马斯·杰斐逊和詹姆斯·门罗的支持。

**杰斐逊**：照您的提议，等于是叫弗吉尼亚帮着南卡罗来纳还债。

**门罗**：而南卡罗来纳的债，抵弗吉尼亚三倍。

**汉密尔顿**：*（对门罗）* 那是在为弗吉尼亚赢得自由的当口欠下的。二位先生，十三州的利益若继续这样相争，独立便永远立不稳当。我以爱国者之名恳请二位，莫再为党派之利争执，虚掷国家的光阴。我们是一个国家的营造者；让我们把根基筑牢，筑在荣誉这块基石上。世界各国都在看着我们。任他们讥笑我们年轻，任他们讥笑我们贫穷，但绝不能容他们在我们的诚信上抹一丝黑。

**杰斐逊**：您申张的只是国家的荣誉；我申张的，还有个人的权利。

**门罗**：您可意识到，人民的权利正系于一线？

===Original===

**Hamilton**：*（To **Monroe**.）* The right of the people is the right to cast aside personal interests for the greater good of the nation. The only safety for any people is in a government that can command the respect of the world. *（Striking the table.）*

**Monroe**：*（Angrily.）* You mean a Monarchy?

**Hamilton**：I mean a Republic.

**Monroe**：*（Rising, coming to L. corner of table and pounding it.）* George Washington is trying to set up a Monarchy and make himself King.

**Hamilton**：*（Rising and crossing to R. with **Monroe**.）* Take this chair. I think you’ll be more comfortable.

**Monroe**：*（Ignoring the chair.）* Suppose the government should pay the states’ debts, the men who lent the money wouldn’t get it. *（**Hamilton** about to interrupt.）* You know as well as I do that the patriots who put up the money to carry on the war have long ago given up all hope of ever being paid by the bankrupt states.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：*（对门罗）* 人民的权利，正是撇开一己之利、成全国家大义的权利。任何人民唯一的安全，系于一个能赢得世人敬重的政府。*（一掌拍在桌上）*

**门罗**：*（动怒）* 您指的是君主政体？

**汉密尔顿**：我指的是共和政体。

**门罗**：*（起身，走到桌子左角，捶着桌面）* 乔治·华盛顿就想立君主政体，自立为王。

**汉密尔顿**：*（起身，随门罗走到右边）* 坐这把椅子吧。我想您会舒服些。

**门罗**：*（不理会那把椅子）* 就算政府把各州的债都担下来，当初借钱的人也拿不着钱。*（汉密尔顿正要插话）* 您跟我一样清楚：那些掏钱支撑战争的爱国志士，早就断了从破产各州手里讨还的指望。

===Original===

**Hamilton**：That is exactly﻿—

**Monroe**：Their claims have passed into other hands, sold for a song.

**Hamilton**：*（C.）* But the claims remain﻿—and a promise to pay is a promise to pay.

**Jefferson**：*（Rising and with firmness.）* Citizen Hamilton, I am pledged to the people.

**Monroe**：We cannot pick the pockets of the man who trusts us in order to pay another man’s debts.

**Hamilton**：Gentlemen, this is repudiation﻿—the last stage of national humiliation.

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：那恰正是——

**门罗**：那些债权早换了主人，贱得跟白送一样卖掉了。

**汉密尔顿**：*（台中）* 可债权还在——欠债要还，终究是欠债要还。

**杰斐逊**：*（起身，语气坚决）* 汉密尔顿公民，我向人民立过誓。

**门罗**：我们总不能掏信任我们的人的腰包，去还另一个人的债。

**汉密尔顿**：二位先生，这是赖账——是一个国家坠入耻辱的最后一级。

===Original===

**Jefferson**：I have tried to see your point of view. Citizen Monroe has tried to see it.

**Monroe**：Yes, I’ve tried.

**Jefferson**：You have appealed to Congress many times, and have always been defeated.

**Hamilton**：The last time by a majority of two.

**Jefferson**：The majority was small, it is true﻿—*（with a touch of courtliness）* mainly owing, I fear, to the eloquence of your address, Citizen Hamilton.

**Hamilton**：*（Responding with a profound bow.）* From Thomas Jefferson that is indeed praise. But I fear my eloquence has interfered with our friendly chat, and that was really what you came for.

===Chinese===

**杰斐逊**：我曾设法体察您的立场。门罗公民也曾设法体察。

**门罗**：是啊，我试过。

**杰斐逊**：您向国会申诉过多少回，回回都败下阵来。

**汉密尔顿**：上回只差两票。

**杰斐逊**：多数确实微弱——*（带着几分温文）* 我怕主要是拜您那篇演说的雄辩所赐，汉密尔顿公民。

**汉密尔顿**：*（深深一躬作答）* 出自托马斯·杰斐逊之口，这实在是夸奖了。只怕我的雄辩搅了二位的友好叙谈——那才是二位真正的来意。

===Original===

**Jefferson**：Some other time﻿—*（About to turn up stage.）*

**Monroe**：Well, Jefferson, time is short﻿—

**Jefferson**：This discussion having arisen, I find myself diffident in seeking your cooperation on another matter.

**Hamilton**：Gentlemen, I beg that you will not deprive me of so great a privilege﻿—pray sit down again. *（They sit.）* You are sure you are comfortable in that chair, Mr. Monroe?

**Monroe**：*（Seated in front of sofa.）* Yes, thank you. I was never made to occupy a throne.

**Hamilton**：*（About to respond but controls himself and sitting in chair R. of small table C.）* Gentlemen, I am at your service.

===Chinese===

**杰斐逊**：改日再叙——*（正要往台后转）*

**门罗**：嗐，杰斐逊，时辰不等人——

**杰斐逊**：既是起了这场争论，再就另一件事求您协力，我未免难以启齿。

**汉密尔顿**：二位先生，千万别叫我失了这份殊荣——快请再坐。*（二人落座）* 门罗先生，那把椅子坐得果真舒服吗？

**门罗**：*（在沙发前坐下）* 舒服，多谢。我天生就不是坐宝座的料。

**汉密尔顿**：*（正要回敬，忍住了，在中小桌右侧的椅子上坐下）* 二位先生，愿听吩咐。

===Original===

**Jefferson**：*（Seated again in chair between tables.）* You know that the Residence Bill must be voted on without further delay.

**Hamilton**：I understand that immediate decision is necessary.

**Jefferson**：I will not disguise from you that I consider the geographical position of the Capital a very vital matter.

**Hamilton**：Undoubtedly.

**Jefferson**：And we do not underrate your influence, not only with the President but with Congress, in arriving at a decision.

**Hamilton**：That decision, gentlemen, is a very grave and serious matter.

===Chinese===

**杰斐逊**：*（在两桌之间的椅子上重新坐定）* 你们知道，定居法案不容再拖，必须付诸表决。

**汉密尔顿**：我明白，必须即刻定夺。

**杰斐逊**：我不向您隐瞒：首都的地理位置，依我看是件性命攸关的大事。

**汉密尔顿**：毫无疑问。

**杰斐逊**：我们也决不敢低估您的影响力——不论在总统跟前，还是在国会，于定夺此事都举足轻重。

**汉密尔顿**：这个决定，二位先生，是件极为重大、极需慎重的事。

===Original===

**Monroe**：It certainly is.

**Hamilton**：But I think we ought to find no difficulty in reaching an agreement.

**Monroe**：Now, that’s talking sense.

**Jefferson**：I am pleased to find that you are willing to meet us in the matter.

**Hamilton**：We should have no difficulty, because, to me﻿—and surely to all who have gone into the matter as deeply and seriously as we have﻿—there can be only one possible location for the Capital.

**Monroe**：And that is?

===Chinese===

**门罗**：可不是嘛。

**汉密尔顿**：不过我想，我们达成一致，理当毫无困难。

**门罗**：这才像句明白话。

**杰斐逊**：得悉您愿在此事上与我们相就，我很欣慰。

**汉密尔顿**：我们理当毫无困难。因为在我看来——想必也在一切同我们一样深入、一样郑重地掂量过此事的人看来——首都可能的所在，只有一处。

**门罗**：是哪儿？

===Original===

**Hamilton**：Albany. *（A distinct ejaculation is heard from **Schuyler** outside. He disappears to L.）* Excuse me. I thought I heard my father-in-law calling.

**Jefferson**：Albany!

**Monroe**：That old Dutch town!

**Jefferson**：And why Albany?

**Hamilton**：*（Returning to C. behind small table.）* Because *（oratorically; imitating **Schuyler**）* it commands the commerce of the four corners of the earth.

**Jefferson**：But I fear you do not consider﻿—

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：奥尔巴尼。*（外面传来斯凯勒一声清晰的惊呼，随即人影消失在左边）* 恕我走神。我还当听见我岳父在唤我。

**杰斐逊**：奥尔巴尼！

**门罗**：那个老荷兰镇子！

**杰斐逊**：为何是奥尔巴尼？

**汉密尔顿**：*（回到小桌后台中）* 因为——*（学起演说腔，模仿斯凯勒）* 它扼着天下四方的商路。

**杰斐逊**：只怕您没有考量到——

===Original===

**Hamilton**：I know much may be said in favor of New York and Philadelphia﻿—but﻿—

**Monroe**：You talk as if Albany, New York, and Philadelphia were the only places in the Union, sir.

**Hamilton**：*（Surprised.）* Had you any other place in mind?

**Monroe**：Hasn’t the South as much claim as the North?

**Hamilton**：Certainly not, sir. The South could not possibly be considered.

**Jefferson**：Why not, sir?

===Chinese===

**汉密尔顿**：我知道，替纽约和费城说话的理由不少——不过——

**门罗**：先生，听您这话，倒像合众国里只有奥尔巴尼、纽约、费城三处地方了。

**汉密尔顿**：*（讶然）* 二位心里另有别的地方？

**门罗**：南方的资格，难道不跟北方一样？

**汉密尔顿**：当然不一样，先生。南方压根儿不在考虑之列。

**杰斐逊**：为何不在，先生？

===Original===

**Monroe**：What’s your reason for the North, except you’re a Northerner yourself?

**Hamilton**：*（Oratorically.）* Why, gentlemen *（pulls chair R. of small table to back of small table and comes to R. of small table）*, all the great events connected with our glorious struggle for independence cluster about the North. *（**Schuyler’s** face expresses amazement and delight. **Hamilton** lightly touches his fingers, as he enumerates the following.）* It was here in this city﻿—in Philadelphia﻿—that was signed that immortal document from your hand, Mr. Jefferson﻿—the Declaration of Independence.

**Monroe**：What’s that got to do with it?

**Hamilton**：It was in New York that Washington took his oath of office. *（**Schuyler** gives great signs of approval.）* It was there that the government had its beginning, and﻿—and﻿—there’s not a﻿—there’s no reason on earth why the South should have it anyway.

**Monroe**：*（Rising and going R.）* The South will make a damned good fight for it.

**Jefferson**：*（Rising.）* Is that your final word, Mr. Hamilton?

===Chinese===

**门罗**：您向着北方，除了您自己是个北方人，还有什么道理？

**汉密尔顿**：*（学起演说腔）* 嗯，二位先生*（把小桌右边的椅子拉到桌后，走到小桌右边）*，凡同我们光荣的独立大业相系的大事，尽聚于北方。*（斯凯勒面露惊诧与欣喜。汉密尔顿逐条数说，轻叩手指记数。）* 正是在这座城——在费城——签署了出自您手的那份不朽文献，杰斐逊先生——《独立宣言》。

**门罗**：这跟选址何干？

**汉密尔顿**：华盛顿正是在纽约宣誓就职的。*（斯凯勒连连点头称是）* 政府正是在那里开的端，还有——还有——总没有——总没有半点儿道理叫南方得了去。

**门罗**：*（起身走向右边）* 南方为这个，是会拼上一场硬仗的。

**杰斐逊**：*（起身）* 这就是您的最后态度吗，汉密尔顿先生？

===Original===

**Hamilton**：My decision in this matter, Mr. Jefferson, is just as irrevocable as that of yours and Senator Monroe regarding my bill. *（**Monroe** and **Jefferson** exchange a glance which **Hamilton** observes.）* You’ll excuse me. I’m sure I heard the General calling. *（Going to window C.）* I’m afraid of the night air for your gout, father-in-law. You’d better be getting home.

**Schuyler**：Good evening, gentlemen.

**Jefferson**：Good evening, Citizen Schuyler.

**Monroe**：Good evening.

**Schuyler**：*（Passing across toward door up R.）* I won’t interrupt you. I’ll just toddle along.
