**贝特西**：受得住？那自然，因为我知道那不是真的。

===Original===
**Hamilton**：*（Rising.）* How do you know it isn’t true?

**Betsy**：What do you mean? Because I know you, Alexander.

**Hamilton**：*（Advancing to her.）* Do you know me﻿—do you?

**Betsy**：Tell me what you mean?

===Chinese===
**汉密尔顿**：*（起身）* 你怎么知道那不是真的？

**贝特西**：你这是什么意思？因为我了解你呀，亚历山大。

**汉密尔顿**：*（向她逼近）* 你了解我——当真了解吗？

**贝特西**：告诉我，你这是什么意思？

===Original===
**Hamilton**：*（Taking her by the arms.）* Why don’t you suspect me﻿—why do you trust me?

**Betsy**：Don’t﻿—don’t! Tell me what it is. You can prove it isn’t true.

**Hamilton**：I can, but I dare not.

**Betsy**：You dare not?

===Chinese===
**汉密尔顿**：*（抓住她的双臂）* 你为什么不疑我——为什么这样信我？

**贝特西**：别——别这样！告诉我，到底怎么了。你能证明那不是真的。

**汉密尔顿**：我能，可我不敢。

**贝特西**：你不敢？

===Original===
**Hamilton**：I dare not tell the truth. I did pay certain sums of money.

**Betsy**：What for? What did you pay it for?

**Hamilton**：To keep from the light something of which I am ashamed﻿—to hide something shameful from you.

===Chinese===
**汉密尔顿**：我不敢说出真相。我确实付过几笔钱。

**贝特西**：为什么？那钱是做什么用的？

**汉密尔顿**：为了把我引以为耻的事捂在暗处——为了把一件见不得人的事瞒着你。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：*（Down L.）* Alexander, have you no feeling for your wife? Betsy, go﻿—

**Hamilton**：*（Stepping back from **Betsy**.）* Father, it’s better that she should know. Betsy﻿—

**Schuyler**：You’re not going to drag that woman﻿—

**Jay**：Oh! *（A moment of dead awful silence.）*

===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：*（走到左前）* 亚历山大，你就全不顾念你的妻子吗？贝特西，走——

**汉密尔顿**：*（从贝特西身边退开一步）* 父亲，还是让她知道的好。贝特西——

**斯凯勒**：你总不能把那个女人也——

**杰伊**：哦！*（死一般可怕的静默，片刻）*

===Original===
**Betsy**：*（Turning dazed to **Schuyler**.）* It’s not a woman? *（He bows his head. She turns to **Hamilton** and sees the guilt on his face.）* Oh!﻿—

**Schuyler**：Betsy, my girl!

**Betsy**：*（Lifting her head, dazed, but with a sense of pride.）* Does anyone else know?

**Schuyler**：*（Quietly.）* Mr. Jefferson, that is all.

===Chinese===
**贝特西**：*（怔怔地转向斯凯勒）* 不是……一个女人？*（他低下头。她转向汉密尔顿，看见他脸上的愧色）* 哦！——

**斯凯勒**：贝特西，我的孩子！

**贝特西**：*（抬起头，神情恍惚，却带着一份自尊）* 还有别人知道吗？

**斯凯勒**：*（低声）* 只有杰斐逊先生，就这些了。

===Original===
**Betsy**：*（Looking at **Monroe** and **Jay**.）* Will they tell?

**Jay
						Monroe**：No.

**Betsy**：*（Slowly.）* Then it need never be known.

===Chinese===
**贝特西**：*（看看门罗和杰伊）* 他们会说出去吗？

**杰伊
						门罗**：不会。

**贝特西**：*（缓缓地）* 那么，这事就永远不必让人知道了。

===Original===
**Giles**：Then you withdraw your Bill?

**Hamilton**：*（With a great sudden outburst.）* No﻿—no﻿—I can’t do it. I’ve done with bargains. I’ve bargained with my conscience long enough. I’m covered with shame and remorse, but I can’t stand in the mud and barter what I believe to be America’s honor. My Bill stands. Get out and tell your story, and I’ll tell mine.

**Giles**：What’ll you tell?

**Hamilton**：The truth! *（Picking up letters.）* The whole degrading, sordid truth. In tomorrow’s newspaper I’ll publish every fragment of evidence in connection with my disgrace with this woman. If there is to be dishonor, it shall fall on me and not on this administration. I sacrifice my office﻿—I sacrifice my wife﻿—but, by God, Betsy, I can’t sell my country. *（With a peremptory gesture to **Giles**.）* Now go!!!

===Chinese===
**贾尔斯**：那么，你撤回你的州债法案了？

**汉密尔顿**：*（勃然爆发）* 不——不——我办不到。交易，我受够了。我同自己的良心讨价还价，已经讨得够久了。我浑身蒙满了羞耻和悔恨，可我不能站在泥里，拿我所信奉的美利坚的荣誉去做买卖。我的州债法案照样立在那儿。滚出去，讲你们的故事去吧，我也要讲我的。

**贾尔斯**：你要讲什么？

**汉密尔顿**：真相！*（抓起信件）* 全部丢人的、肮脏的真相。明天的报纸上，我要把我同这个女人的丑事的每一片证据，统统公之于众。倘若注定要蒙羞，这羞辱就落在我一人身上，决不落在这个政府身上。我牺牲我的官职——我牺牲我的妻子——可是，苍天在上，贝特西，我不能出卖我的国家。*（对贾尔斯做了个不容分说的手势）* 走，马上！！！

## Act IV / 第四幕

===Original===
Scene.﻿—A room in **Hamilton’s** house; the next morning. It is a lofty anteroom, with very large folding doors centre, which, when opened, disclose a large reception room beyond. There are doors down right and left and a window up right. The room is severe in its Colonial dignity, very large panels being either side of the folding door, and on the walls right and left. The general tone is of a yellowish cream relieved with gray. A harpsichord is on stage down right centre and a chair in front of it. Black console tables with a pair of marble vases stand against the walls right and left of centre doors at back. Hanging above the consoles in black oval frames are two old-fashioned pictures of flowers. A three-piece Chippendale settee is against the wall left above the door. Lace curtains and blue rep hangings on window up right. When centre doors are opened you see a large handsome mantel at back with windows right and left hung with red rep curtains. Two small square tables in front of windows, and a large mahogany table in centre in front of fireplace. Ornaments on mantel and vases on small tables. The doors are closed.
===Chinese===
场景——汉密尔顿宅中的一间屋子；次日早晨。这是一间高敞的前厅，正中是两扇宽大的对折门，门开处显出后面一间宽敞的会客厅。左右下方各有一道门，右上方是一扇窗。屋子带着殖民地式的庄重气派，对折门两侧与左右墙上皆是宽大的镶板。整体色调为奶黄，间以灰色提亮。右中偏前摆着一架拨弦古钢琴，琴前设一张椅子。后中央门左右两侧墙边各靠一张黑色橱桌，桌上置一对大理石花瓶。橱桌上方悬着黑框椭圆的两幅古式花卉画。三件套的齐彭代尔长椅靠在门上方的左墙边。右上的窗挂蕾丝窗帘与蓝色棱纹平布帘。中央门打开时，可见后墙一座华美的大壁炉，左右有窗，垂着红色棱纹平布窗帘。窗前各一张小方桌，壁炉正前方居中是一张大桃花心木桌。壁炉上摆着饰物，小桌上供着花瓶。各门均闭。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：Where’s Betsy?

**Angelica**：*（R. of him.）* Still in her room.

**Schuyler**：I wish Alexander would come.

**Angelica**：He’s still locked in his study.

**Schuyler**：He’s been writing since early morning. Can’t you get him out?

**Angelica**：I’ve tried. I told him there were people waiting to see him. He called out, “Let them wait.”
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：贝特西呢？

**安杰莉卡**：*（在他右侧）* 还在她房里。

**斯凯勒**：亚历山大要是肯出来就好了。

**安杰莉卡**：他还把自己锁在书房里。

**斯凯勒**：他从一大早写到现在。你就不能把他请出来？

**安杰莉卡**：试过了。我告诉他有人等着见他。他隔着门喊：「让他们等。」

===Original===
**Schuyler**：But this room *（pointing to doors up stage）* is full of men﻿—Senators, Congressmen, and heaven knows who besides. It’s not like him to run away.

**Angelica**：He won’t run away.

**Schuyler**：The thing’s done. He’s got to face the music.

**Angelica**：He’ll face it.

**Schuyler**：How does Betsy seem now?

**Angelica**：She’s so deadly calm. I wish she’d cry or rave﻿—be more human.
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：可这间屋子*（指向上方的门）*里坐满了人——参议员、众议员，还有天知道些什么人。躲避退缩可不像他的为人。

**安杰莉卡**：他不会躲避的。

**斯凯勒**：事情已经做下了。他只得出来承受这一切。

**安杰莉卡**：他会承受的。

**斯凯勒**：贝特西现在怎么样？

**安杰莉卡**：她镇定得吓人。我倒盼着她哭一场、闹一场——更像个人样。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：She’s determined to go?

**Angelica**：Yes, I can do nothing with her.

**Schuyler**：*（Looking at paper.）* It’s a bad business﻿—a bad business. *（Putting paper behind him quickly.）*

**Angelica**：*（R. C.）* You needn’t hide that paper. I’m not ashamed of it.

**Schuyler**：*（Surprised.）* You’re not?

**Angelica**：*（Coming to him.）* Oh, father, isn’t he wonderful?

**Schuyler**：Who?

**Angelica**：Alexander.
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：她拿定主意要走了？

**安杰莉卡**：是啊，我说什么也劝不动她。

**斯凯勒**：*（看着手里的报纸）* 这件事糟透了——糟透了。*（急忙把报纸藏到身后）*

**安杰莉卡**：*（台中偏右）* 用不着藏那份报纸。我不觉得它丢人。

**斯凯勒**：*（诧异）* 你不觉得？

**安杰莉卡**：*（走到他跟前）* 哦，父亲，他难道不了不起吗？

**斯凯勒**：谁？

**安杰莉卡**：亚历山大。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：Well﻿—er﻿—

**Angelica**：Oh, I wish he were my husband﻿—I should be proud of him.

**Schuyler**：Angelica, if you’d only been a man﻿—if you’d been my eldest son instead of my eldest daughter, I’d have taken you by the hand and called you a fine fellow *（shakes hands with her）*, but those are hardly the proper sentiments for a young married woman.

**Angelica**：If he were my husband, I’d show him how a woman can forgive.
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：这个——呃——

**安杰莉卡**：哦，他要是我丈夫该多好——我定要以他为荣。

**斯凯勒**：安杰莉卡，你要是个男人就好了——你要是我的长子而不是长女，我就要握住你的手，称你一声好汉*（与她握手）*。可这样的心思，实在不是一个年轻的已婚妇人该有的。

**安杰莉卡**：他要是我丈夫，我就让他看看，女人是怎样宽恕的。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：But he isn’t your husband, and that makes all the difference. Don’t be hard on Betsy. It’s easy enough for one woman to forgive another woman’s husband. *（Enter **Zekiel** door L.）*

**Zekiel**：Speakah Mullenburg an’ Mistah Morris has jes’ come.

**Schuyler**：Did you say Mr. Hamilton was engaged?

**Zekiel**：Yessah, but they said lack de odders dat dey’d wait, so I done showed ’em into de room wid de rest of ’em.

**Angelica**：Very well, Zekiel.

**Zekiel**：They ain’ goin’ to do nothin’ to Mars’ Hamilton, is dey, sah?

**Schuyler**：No, no, no!

**Zekiel**：*（Going out.）* Wal, I done wish dey wouldn’t all stan’ roun’ waitin’ for him. *（Exit, L.）*
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：可他不是你的丈夫，这一层就差到了底。别对贝特西太苛刻。一个女人要宽恕另一个女人的丈夫，总是容易的。*（泽基尔自左门上）*

**泽基尔**：米伦伯格先生跟莫里斯先生刚刚到啦。

**斯凯勒**：你没说汉密尔顿先生正忙着吗？

**泽基尔**：说了老爷，可他们说跟别位一样愿意等，我就把他们领进那间屋，跟其余的人搁一块儿了。

**安杰莉卡**：很好，泽基尔。

**泽基尔**：他们不会对汉密尔顿老爷怎么样吧，是吧，老爷？

**斯凯勒**：不会，不会，不会！

**泽基尔**：*（正要出去）* 嗐，我就盼着他们别全都围着等他。*（自左下）*

===Original===
**Angelica**：*（Up stage R. C.）* I’m glad I’m not your eldest son, after all, father.

**Schuyler**：What is it now, Angelica?

**Angelica**：*（Indicating toward inner room C.）* If these are fair samples of men, I’m glad I’m not one of the tribe. All waiting around to kick a man when he’s down.

**Schuyler**：Oh, there you are, Betsy! Going out?

**Betsy**：*（R. C.）* Yes, I’m going, father. Has Alexander come down?

**Schuyler**：Not yet. Where are you going?

**Betsy**：I’m going to Albany, father, to wait for you. I’m going home.
===Chinese===
**安杰莉卡**：*（右中偏后）* 父亲，说到底，我倒庆幸自己不是您的长子。

**斯凯勒**：这又是怎么了，安杰莉卡？

**安杰莉卡**：*（指内室方向）* 要是这些人就算是男人的像样样品，我庆幸自己不属于这一族。一个个守在那儿，专等一个人倒了，再上去踹一脚。

**斯凯勒**：哦，你来了，贝特西！要出去？

**贝特西**：*（台中偏右）* 是的，我要走了，父亲。亚历山大下来了吗？

**斯凯勒**：还没有。你要上哪儿去？

**贝特西**：去奥尔巴尼，父亲，去那儿等您。我要回家。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：Won’t you stay and see him?

**Betsy**：Things for him will go on just as if I had not come back from Europe. That is all. He can resume that life. The coach for Albany leaves at noon.

**Schuyler**：*（C.）* You’d better see him, Betsy.

**Betsy**：*（R. C.）* I’ve seen the morning paper.

**Angelica**：*（L. C.）* And so have I﻿—read every word of it﻿—twice.

**Schuyler**：Well, if your sister’s determined to go, you had better get your things together, Angelica, and go with her. She can’t go alone.

**Angelica**：Why don’t *you* go with her?

**Schuyler**：I can’t leave Alexander at a time like this.

**Angelica**：Well, neither can I. Betsy’s the only one who can leave him, so she must go alone.
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：不留下见见他？

**贝特西**：他的日子会照旧过下去，就当我压根没从欧洲回来。就这么回事。他可以接着过那种日子。去奥尔巴尼的马车正午发车。

**斯凯勒**：*（台中）* 你还是见见他好，贝特西。

**贝特西**：*（台中偏右）* 早报我看过了。

**安杰莉卡**：*（台中偏左）* 我也看了——一字不落——看了两遍。

**斯凯勒**：好吧，你妹妹既然决意要走，你最好收拾收拾，安杰莉卡，陪她一起去。她不能独自上路。

**安杰莉卡**：*您*怎么不陪她去？

**斯凯勒**：这种时候，我丢不下亚历山大。

**安杰莉卡**：哼，我也丢不下。既然只有贝特西一个人丢得下他，那她就只好独自走了。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：Come, come, Angelica. You mustn’t talk like that.

**Betsy**：*（Coming to **Schuyler**.）* Don’t, father. I’d much rather go alone. I can’t bear to see or speak to anybody, I’m so ashamed﻿—I can’t even face you.

**Angelica**：*（Stopping her C.）* Betsy, take off that hat and stand by him. Are you going to leave that man?

**Betsy**：Yes.

**Angelica**：Good. He doesn’t need you. He never needed you less. If you can’t appreciate his courage and bravery, you are no fit mate for him. God knows there are few enough men who are willing to sacrifice everything for the truth. If you’re going, go. Go and join those men in there who are all waiting to take a peck at him.
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：得了，得了，安杰莉卡。不许说这样的话。

**贝特西**：*（走到斯凯勒跟前）* 别说了，父亲。我巴不得一个人走。我见不得人，也说不得话——我羞成这样——连您的面都不敢见。

**安杰莉卡**：*（在台中拦住她）* 贝特西，摘下那顶帽子，站到他身边去。你要离开那个男人？

**贝特西**：是。

**安杰莉卡**：好啊。他用不着你。他从来没有像现在这样用不着你。你既然赏识不了他的胆识和勇气，就配不上做他的伴侣。上帝知道，肯为真相牺牲一切的男人本就没有几个。要走就走吧。去跟里头那帮人凑一块儿——他们正一个个等着啄他一口呢。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：Angelica!

**Angelica**：I know. Alexander’s done a wicked, disgraceful thing. That’s what makes the difference between a human being and a whitewashed saint. I’m a human being myself, and I’m going to wait here for Alexander. *（Enter **Zekiel**, L.）*

**Schuyler**：Well?

**Zekiel**：It’s a lady callin’, Mis’ Betsy.

**Betsy**：*（Interested.）* Who is it?

**Zekiel**：*（Lamely.）* It’s a﻿—Mrs. Reynolds, ma’am.

**Schuyler
							Angelica**：*（Together; indignantly.）* What?

**Betsy**：Tell her Mr. Hamilton is out.

**Zekiel**：She wants to see you, Mis’ Betsy.

**Angelica**：*（Indignant.）* I never in all my life!

**Schuyler**：*（About to go toward L.）* I’ll soon settle her.

**Betsy**：Wait a moment, father. I’ll see her.
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：安杰莉卡！

**安杰莉卡**：我知道。亚历山大做了一件伤天害理、见不得人的事。可这正是一个活人和一个粉刷得雪白的圣徒之间的区别。我自己是个活人，我就守在这儿等亚历山大。*（泽基尔自左上）*

**斯凯勒**：什么事？

**泽基尔**：有位太太求见，贝特西太太。

**贝特西**：*（关切）* 是谁？

**泽基尔**：*（吞吞吐吐）* 是位——雷诺兹夫人，太太。

**斯凯勒、安杰莉卡**：*（同声，愤然）* 什么？

**贝特西**：就说汉密尔顿先生不在家。

**泽基尔**：她要见的是您，贝特西太太。

**安杰莉卡**：*（愤慨）* 我活了这辈子，就没见过这种事！

**斯凯勒**：*（正要往左去）* 我这就去打发她。

**贝特西**：等一等，父亲。我来见她。

===Original===
**Angelica**：No, no, Zekiel.

**Betsy**：*（Firmly.）* Show her in, Zekiel. *（**Zekiel** exits L.）*

**Angelica**：Do you mean to say you’re going to see that disgraceful woman?

**Betsy**：Yes. Will you and father go away, please?

**Angelica**：But why? Why do you want to see her?

**Betsy**：I want to see what she looks like. Perhaps there’s something of the human being in me, too.
===Chinese===
**安杰莉卡**：不，不，泽基尔。

**贝特西**：*（决然）* 带她进来，泽基尔。*（泽基尔自左下）*

**安杰莉卡**：你难道真要见那个不体面的女人？

**贝特西**：真要见。请您和父亲回避一下，好吗？

**安杰莉卡**：可这是为什么？你为什么要见她？

**贝特西**：我想看看她长什么模样。或许，我心里也还有几分活人的心肠。

===Original===
**Schuyler**：Come on, Angelica. *（**Angelica** crosses to R. and opens door.）* I’ve got two daughters and your dear mother at home, but I’ll be hanged if I understand the first thing about women.

**Zekiel**：Mrs. Reynolds.

**Mrs. Reynolds**：*（L. C.）* You’re Mrs. Hamilton, aren’t you? Mighty nice of you to see me.

**Betsy**：What do you want?

**Mrs. Reynolds**：Why﻿—of course, you’ve seen the newspaper.

**Betsy**：Yes.
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：走吧，安杰莉卡。*（安杰莉卡走向右侧，开门）* 我养了两个女儿，家里还有你们亲爱的母亲，可女人到底是怎么回事，我要是能懂得一星半点，把我吊死都行。

**泽基尔**：雷诺兹夫人到。

**雷诺兹夫人**：*（左中）* 您就是汉密尔顿夫人吧？您肯见我，真是太好了。

**贝特西**：你想做什么？

**雷诺兹夫人**：这个——当然了，报纸您已经看过了。

**贝特西**：看过了。

===Original===
**Mrs. Reynolds**：*（With conscious pride.）* Yes, it’s made quite a stir, hasn’t it? I came because I thought you might feel badly about it. I thought you might feel angry with him.

**Betsy**：With whom?

**Mrs. Reynolds**：Why, with Mr. Hamilton. I don’t see how anyone could﻿—he’s so nice, but﻿—

**Betsy**：What is it you want to say?

**Mrs. Reynolds**：Well, I wouldn’t like you to be cross with him. You mustn’t blame him because it wasn’t his fault.

**Betsy**：Whose fault was it?

**Mrs. Reynolds**：Well, it was mine in the end, but at first it was Reynolds’.
===Chinese===
**雷诺兹夫人**：*（不无得意）* 是啊，闹得满城风雨了，是不是？我来，是想着您心里也许不好受。想着您也许在生他的气。

**贝特西**：生谁的气？

**雷诺兹夫人**：嗐，生汉密尔顿先生的气呗。我不明白怎么会有人生他的气——他那么和气，可是——

**贝特西**：你到底想说什么？

**雷诺兹夫人**：这个，我不愿意您跟他怄气。您不能怪他，因为那不是他的错。

**贝特西**：那是谁的错？

**雷诺兹夫人**：说到底，是我的错，可起头是雷诺兹的错。

===Original===
**Betsy**：Reynolds?

**Mrs. Reynolds**：Yes, Reynolds arranged it because some of his friends﻿—political gentlemen﻿—wanted to get Mr. Hamilton talked about. And of course when I went I hadn’t an idea what he was like﻿—and when I found he was so nice, I half wished I hadn’t said I’d do it. But I’d given my word, you see﻿—and then you were away, and I’d never seen you. *（Coming closer to look at her.）* *You’re* pretty, too, aren’t you, only in a different way from me, and older. Don’t you hate to think of growing old?
===Chinese===
**贝特西**：雷诺兹？

**雷诺兹夫人**：是啊，是雷诺兹安排的，因为他有几个朋友——政界上的老爷们——想让汉密尔顿先生成为人们闲话的谈资。我上门去的时候，压根不知道他是怎样的人——等发觉他那么和气，倒有一半懊悔不该应承干这件事了。可我已经点了头，您瞧——再说您又不在家，我从没见过您。*（凑近端详她）* 您也生得俊，是不是？只是跟我不是一个路数，年纪也大些。一想到人要变老，您就不嫌烦吗？

===Original===
**Betsy**：Mrs. Reynolds, have you no sense of right and wrong?

**Mrs. Reynolds**：Oh, yes, I know﻿—I know when I’m doing wrong﻿—but you see I have nobody to keep me straight. *（A little to C.）*

**Betsy**：Do you realize that you have broken this home and ruined a man’s life? Isn’t the thought of that enough to keep you straight?
===Chinese===
**贝特西**：雷诺兹夫人，你就没有是非之心吗？

**雷诺兹夫人**：哦，有的，我知道——干坏事的时候我知道——可您瞧，没有人管着我走正道。*（往台中挪了挪）*

**贝特西**：你可知道你拆散了这个家，毁了一个男人的一生？一想到这个，还不足以让你走正道吗？

===Original===
**Mrs. Reynolds**：*（Turning and coming back.）* Oh, you’re not going to leave him! You can’t do that. That’s why I came, because I thought you might be cross with him.

**Betsy**：Have you no decency? Your name flaming in the newspaper﻿—your shame on the lips of every man and woman in the city!
===Chinese===
**雷诺兹夫人**：*（回过身来）* 哦，您不是要离开他吧！您不能那样。我就是为这个来的，我怕您跟他怄气。

**贝特西**：你就没有一丝廉耻吗？你的名字在报纸上烧得一片火红——你的丑事挂在全城男女的嘴边上！

===Original===
**Mrs. Reynolds**：Well, yes, of course it is bad in a sense, but then it’s different for me to what it is for you, because it does give me a sort of a position. You see I’ve never had any position before, and now my name being in the paper coupled with Alexander Hamilton﻿—

**Betsy**：*（Stifled.）* Oh﻿—

**Mrs. Reynolds**：It’ll make Reynolds behave a good deal better to know me, I know. Mean old thing! Of course you’re good, with a good husband, and you don’t understand. I suppose everything depends on the way you’re brought up, doesn’t it? I don’t mean to be wicked﻿—I wish I wasn’t!
===Chinese===
**雷诺兹夫人**：嗐，是啊，从一方面讲当然是坏了名声，可这事对我跟对您不一样，因为它到底给了我一个身份。您瞧，我从前什么身份也没有，如今我的名字登在报纸上，还跟亚历山大·汉密尔顿连在一块儿——

**贝特西**：*（透不过气来）* 哦——

**雷诺兹夫人**：我知道，这一来雷诺兹知道我的分量，就会规矩多了。吝啬的老东西！您当然是好人，有个好丈夫，您不懂。我猜什么事都看是怎么教养出来的，是不是？我不是存心使坏——我要是不坏才好呢！

===Original===
**Betsy**：Good morning, Mrs. Reynolds.

**Mrs. Reynolds**：*（Naively.）* Oh, that means I’m to go. But you won’t leave him, will you?

**Betsy**：We have nothing more to discuss.
===Chinese===
**贝特西**：早安，雷诺兹夫人。

**雷诺兹夫人**：*（天真地）* 哦，这是叫我走了。可您不会离开他的，对吧？

**贝特西**：我们之间没有什么可谈的了。

===Original===
**Mrs. Reynolds**：*（Coming beside her and placing hand on chair.）* Mrs. Hamilton, I didn’t want to come here today and face you. It took some courage, I can tell you. But when I saw his confession this morning, I reckoned that took some courage too. I knew you had come home and that you’d see it all in the paper, and I made up my mind that you should know it was all a planned thing. I was set on to get him, anyway; but when I saw him and spoke to him and he thought I was in trouble and was so kind to me, I just fell in love with him and I didn’t mean to let him go.
===Chinese===
**雷诺兹夫人**：*（走到她身旁，一只手搭上椅背）* 汉密尔顿夫人，我今天本不想上这儿来见您。不瞒您说，这得有点儿勇气。可今天早晨我看见他那份自白，我琢磨着，那也得有点儿勇气。我知道您回家了，知道您会在报上看到全部经过，我就打定了主意：得让您知道，这整件事都是设好的局。不管怎么说，是人家撺掇我去勾他的；可见了他、跟他说上了话，见他把我想成遭了难的、对我那么和气，我就真的爱上他了，舍不得放他走。

===Original===
**Betsy**：How can you stand there and tell me that?

**Mrs. Reynolds**：Because I don’t want you to be hard on him. He’s a good man﻿—but I made up my mind that he shouldn’t get away from me, so he isn’t to blame, is he?
===Chinese===
**贝特西**：你怎么能站在那儿对我说出这种话？

**雷诺兹夫人**：因为我不愿意您对他太狠心。他是个好人——是我打定了主意不放他脱身，所以不能怪他，是不是？

===Original===
**Schuyler**：Alexander has left his room. I think it would be better if I let this young woman out by the side door. This way, please.

**Mrs. Reynolds**：*（Crossing, turns to **Betsy**.）* You won’t leave him, will you?

**Betsy**：Please go.

**Mrs. Reynolds**：If you only knew more about﻿—women, you’d forgive him.
===Chinese===
**斯凯勒**：亚历山大出书房了。我看还是让我领这位年轻太太从边门出去的好。这边请。

**雷诺兹夫人**：*（走过场时回身对贝特西）* 您不会离开他的，对吧？

**贝特西**：请走吧。

**雷诺兹夫人**：您要是多懂得些——女人的事，就会宽恕他了。

===Original===
**Angelica**：*（Coming down to **Betsy**.）* Did you hear what that woman said?

**Betsy**：Yes.

**Angelica**：I think God put those words into her mouth for you to hear. *（Enter **Hamilton**, L. He carries the Bill on which he has been working. After a moment’s pause.）* Alexander﻿—Betsy﻿—*（**Hamilton** puts his hand up and silences her, looking at **Betsy** as though to say “Let Betsy speak.” **Betsy** avoids looking at him and goes off R., hurriedly, as though she feared she might break down. Going to him in tears.）* Oh, Alexander, you’ve wronged her cruelly, but you stuck to the truth.
===Chinese===
**安杰莉卡**：*（走到贝特西跟前）* 那女人说的话你听见了？

**贝特西**：听见了。

**安杰莉卡**：我想，是上帝把那些话放进她嘴里，说给你听的。*（汉密尔顿自左上。他手中拿着他一直埋头推敲的那部州债法案。片刻停顿）* 亚历山大——贝特西——*（汉密尔顿抬手示意她噤声，眼睛望着贝特西，仿佛在说「让贝特西说」。贝特西避而不看他，匆匆自右下，像是生怕自己撑不住。安杰莉卡含泪走向他）* 哦，亚历山大，你伤透了她的心，可你守住了真相。

===Original===
**Hamilton**：*（Taking her hands.）* It’s a great price to pay﻿—but it was the only way﻿—the only way for me. But oh, Angelica ﻿… I’ve lost Betsy.

**Schuyler**：*（To R. C.）* Alexander, you’ve got to see these people. There’s a whole crowd waiting for you.
===Chinese===
**汉密尔顿**：*（握住她的双手）* 这代价太大了——可这是唯一的办法——对我而言唯一的办法。可是，哦，安杰莉卡……我失去贝特西了。

**斯凯勒**：*（走向右中）* 亚历山大，这些人你总得见。有一大群人等着你呢。

===Original===
**Hamilton**：The wolves are clamoring at the door, eh? Well, they’ll die of overfeeding. Who are they?

**Schuyler**：Jefferson, Monroe, Madison﻿—

**Hamilton**：Ha! Ha! Of course.

**Schuyler**：John Jay, Robert Morris, John Marshall, Robert Livingston, and others besides.

**Hamilton**：My friends, too. My friends of yesterday. Well, they are right. Have you seen them?

**Schuyler**：Not yet. Zekiel tried to get rid of them, but they seem to be waiting with grim determination in there, and won’t be moved.
===Chinese===
**汉密尔顿**：豺狼们在门口嚎着讨吃食，是吧？好啊，撑死他们。都是些什么人？

**斯凯勒**：杰斐逊、门罗、麦迪逊——

**汉密尔顿**：哈！哈！不出所料。

**斯凯勒**：约翰·杰伊、罗伯特·莫里斯、约翰·马歇尔、罗伯特·利文斯顿，另外还有几位。

**汉密尔顿**：也有我的朋友。我昨天的朋友。好吧，他们没错。你见过了？

**斯凯勒**：还没有。泽基尔想法子打发他们，可他们像是横下心守在那儿，说什么也不走。

===Original===
**Hamilton**：In here? *（Going toward doors at back.）* Well, we’ll meet them this way. *（Turns at doors.）* You, dear friends, have been my allies﻿—*（They come to him on either side.）* I release you and I’ll stand alone﻿—not very strong, morally or physically, but we won’t lose our courage. Angelica, dear girl, you have been too good, too indulgent to me, and not fair to Betsy. *（Coming down a step or two with her.）* Go and give her what comfort you can. She will need it﻿—at first.
===Chinese===
**汉密尔顿**：在里头？*（走向后方的门）* 好，那我们就这么见。*（在门前转过身）* 你们，亲爱的朋友们，一向是我的同盟——*（二人左右走到他身边）* 我放你们卸下担子，我独自站着——论道义论气力都不怎么硬朗了，可我们不会失了勇气。安杰莉卡，好姑娘，你对我太好、太纵容，对贝特西却不公道。*（同她走下一两级）* 去吧，能给她多少安慰就给她多少。她会需要的——眼下这一阵。

===Original===
**Angelica**：*（Goes toward door R.）* You’ll stay, father?

**Schuyler**：*（Testily, to cover his emotion.）* Get out, get out! Why should I run away? I’m not a senator, thank God! *（Exit **Angelica**, R.）*
===Chinese===
**安杰莉卡**：*（走向右门）* 您留下吗，父亲？

**斯凯勒**：*（没好气地，借以掩饰激动）* 出去，出去！我跑什么？感谢上帝，我又不是参议员！*（安杰莉卡自右下）*

===Original===
**Hamilton**：*（With hand on door C.）* Father, after this, I shall disappear. I shall probably go back to the West Indies where I was born. I will write to you. You will hear from me, but you won’t see me. Here’s an end to the Federalist Party, and an end to my ambitions. My home is in ruins, but the honor of my office has been saved.
===Chinese===
**汉密尔顿**：*（手扶在中央门上）* 父亲，这事之后，我就销声匿迹了。多半要回西印度群岛去，回到我出生的地方。我会给您写信。您会得到我的音信，却见不到我这个人。联邦党到此完了，我的抱负也到此完了。我的家毁了，可我这个职位的荣誉保住了。

===Original===
**Betsy**：Alexander?

**Hamilton**：*（Turns to her, amazed; comes down and folds her tenderly in his arms.）* Betsy!

**Betsy**：I thought I could be proud and hard, but I can’t, dear﻿—I can’t.

**Hamilton**：*（Kissing her fervently.）* My dear one! *（Kisses her again. Then to **Schuyler**, who has come down R. of them.）* Father, will you take Betsy, till this *（indicating toward C. doors）* is over?

**Betsy**：No, Alexander. Let me stand by you.
===Chinese===
**贝特西**：亚历山大？

**汉密尔顿**：*（惊愕地回过身，走下来，温柔地把她搂进怀里）* 贝特西！

**贝特西**：我原以为自己能硬起心肠、撑住体面，可是我做不到，亲爱的——做不到。

**汉密尔顿**：*（热切地吻她）* 我亲爱的！*（再吻她。然后对已走到他们右首的斯凯勒）* 父亲，您照看着贝特西，等这*（指中央的门）*一场过去，好不好？

**贝特西**：不，亚历山大。让我站在你身边。

===Original===
**Hamilton**：*（Kisses her hand and she moves down R. He becomes a different man and going up he throws open the great centre doors. The room beyond is brilliantly lighted and the entire scene is lighted up. There is a murmur of conversation. The room beyond is filled with men﻿—about a dozen or more; some are seated. Present are **Marshall**, **Morris**, **Jay**, **Jefferson**, **Monroe**, **Livingston**, **Madison**, **Muhlenburg**, etc.）* Gentlemen, good morning! *（He bows and they bow to him.）* I regret that it was necessary for me to keep you waiting, but the publication of the morning paper did not immediately relieve me of my duties as Secretary of the Treasury and I had pressing work. But now, gentlemen, I am here for your consumption.
===Chinese===
**汉密尔顿**：*（吻她的手，她移步至右前方。他像换了一个人，走上前去，猛地推开中央的大门。里间灯火通明，全场为之一亮。人声嗡嗡。里间坐满了人——约莫十几个或更多，有几个落了座。在场者有马歇尔、莫里斯、杰伊、杰斐逊、门罗、利文斯顿、麦迪逊、米伦伯格等）* 诸位先生，早安！*（他鞠躬，众人向他鞠躬）* 很抱歉让诸位久等，只是早晨报纸的刊出并不能立刻卸下我财政部长的职责，我还有几件要紧的公务。不过现在，诸位先生，我听凭诸位发落了。

===Original===
**Jefferson**：Colonel Hamilton, you see before you political adherents, and political opponents﻿—friends and foes. But I have been requested to act as spokesman, and to express﻿—
===Chinese===
**杰斐逊**：汉密尔顿上校，您眼前这些人，有政治上的同道，也有政治上的对手——亦友亦敌。只是大家推我做代表，来向您表达——

===Original===
**Hamilton**：Mr. Jefferson, I know how painful must be the duty which has fallen upon you. Let me relieve you of it. I am glad you are here﻿—both friends and foes. I know the object of your visit. You have come in advance of President Washington’s orders to relieve me of my office. You are justified, and I have no excuses to offer. I am pained and ashamed at this inglorious end of my career. But at least I must still have the courage of my political opinions. Since early morning I have been working to clarify this document. It is the Bill of the Government Assumption of States’ Debts without which this country has no honor. It will now be ignominiously defeated, but it is my conviction that the sense of justice of my opponents will one day compel its adoption, and so, Mr. Jefferson, I deliver it into your hands, sir, for safe keeping.
===Chinese===
**汉密尔顿**：杰斐逊先生，落在您身上的这桩差事有多为难，我知道。就让我替您卸下吧。我很高兴诸位在此——朋友和对手都一样。诸位此行的目的，我也清楚。你们赶在华盛顿总统下令之前来，是要免去我的职务。你们没有错，我也没有任何托词可讲。我的生涯落到这样不体面的收场，我痛心，也羞愧。但至少，我对自己的政见仍得保有勇气。从清早起，我一直在推敲这份文件。这是政府承担各州债务的州债法案——没有它，这个国家便没有荣誉可言。它如今将蒙羞败北，但我深信，我的对手们心中的正义感，终有一日会促使它获得通过。所以，杰斐逊先生，我把它交到您手中，先生，请您代为保管。

===Original===
**Jefferson**：*（Without taking document.）* Alexander Hamilton, you have mistaken our mission. I have been asked to tell you that every man in this room﻿—every man in the Cabinet, every man in the Senate﻿—is anxious to take you by the hand.
===Chinese===
**杰斐逊**：*（未接文件）* 亚历山大·汉密尔顿，您误解了我们的来意。受大家之托，我要告诉您：这间屋子里的每一个人——内阁中的每一个人、参议院中的每一个人——都渴望同您握手。

===Original===
**Monroe**：*（Coming forward and taking his hand.）* Their admiration for your courage has overwhelmed their knowledge of your indiscretion. You need have no fear for your Bill now, Colonel Hamilton; your action will swing the whole country. I trust I know how to appreciate courage and how to acknowledge defeat.
===Chinese===
**门罗**：*（上前握住他的手）* 对您胆气的钦佩，压过了对您不检点的记挂。汉密尔顿上校，如今您不必再为您的法案担忧了；您此举将带动整个国家。我想，我懂得怎样赏识勇气，也懂得怎样认输。

===Original===
**Jay**：Here is Colonel Lear.

**Hamilton**：Colonel Lear. You come from President Washington?

**Lear**：*（Saluting.）* Colonel Hamilton, I have the honor to announce that President Washington has left the executive mansion and is now on his way here to express to you in person the high opinion he holds of your integrity. *（Salutes and retires to **Jay**.）*
===Chinese===
**杰伊**：利尔上校到了。

**汉密尔顿**：利尔上校。您是从华盛顿总统那儿来？

**利尔**：*（行军礼）* 汉密尔顿上校，我荣幸地通告：华盛顿总统已离开官邸，正在来此途中，要当面向您表达他对您操守的高度嘉许。*（行礼，退至杰伊身旁）*

===Original===
**Jefferson**：Citizen Hamilton, our political opinions may differ in the future as in the past, but my impression of your conduct in this matter will remain glowing and unchangeable. It is the bravest thing a man ever did. Your vindication of the honor of the Secretary of the Treasury, the servant of the Nation, at the expense of the honor of Alexander Hamilton, the man, is a display of personal courage that will rouse the admiration of the world. Citizen Hamilton, I am proud to take your hand.
===Chinese===
**杰斐逊**：汉密尔顿公民，今后我们的政见或许仍会同过去一样相左，但您在此事上的作为，在我心中留下的印象将永远炽热、永不改变。这是一个人做过的最勇敢的事。您以亚历山大·汉密尔顿这个人的荣誉为代价，捍卫了财政部长、国家公仆的荣誉——这份个人勇气，将唤起全世界的钦佩。汉密尔顿公民，能同您握手，我引以为荣。

===Original===
**Hamilton**：Mr. Jefferson, Mr. Monroe, gentlemen. I am deeply moved by the expression of your sentiment toward me, but your decision to support this bill has stirred within me a still deeper feeling. By that decision you have established the credit and the honor of the United States. You have opened the floodgates of prosperity, a prosperity that will reach far beyond our present vision, a prosperity that will one day make America the market of the world. This is what I have striven for, and so, gentlemen, I tender you my felicitations and my thanks. *（Turns to **Betsy**.）*
===Chinese===
**汉密尔顿**：杰斐逊先生，门罗先生，诸位先生。诸位对我的这番心意，我深受感动；而诸位支持这项法案的决定，更在我心中激起一股更深的情感。正是这一决定，确立了合众国的信用与荣誉。你们打开了繁荣的闸门——那繁荣将远远超出我们今日的目力，终有一日会使美利坚成为全世界的市场。这正是我奋斗以求的。所以，诸位先生，请接受我的祝贺与我的感谢。*（转向贝特西）*

## Authors’ Preface / 作者序

===Original===
This play is written for the stage. It is written with a desire to convey to the audience that the builders of the foundation of the American Republic were real people, and not merely a procession of nice gray-headed old gentlemen who were mainly occupied in sitting for their portraits to Gilbert Stuart and John Trumbull.

===Chinese===
本剧是为舞台而写的。作者希望借它向观众传达这样一点：美利坚共和国基业的缔造者们是有血有肉的真人，而不只是一列和蔼可亲的白发老绅士，成天主要忙于坐着让吉尔伯特·斯图尔特和约翰·特朗布尔替他们画像。

===Original===
Probably no keen admirer of Alexander Hamilton will be fully satisfied with the play. But the authors console themselves with the reflection that no playwright could do justice to the power and scope of this remarkable man within the limits of an evening’s entertainment. In writing a play dealing with a great historical figure it is necessary to select an incident that brings out boldly the predominant characteristics of the hero. Having decided upon the incident, it is advisable not to befog it by the introduction of other important episodes however much they may redound to the credit of the central figure, or however much you may be tempted to use them. Alexander Hamilton achieved distinction in so many different directions—as a shipping clerk, as a soldier, as a powerful and graceful writer, as an orator, as a tactician, as a master of the financial policy of Nations—that to the casual reader of history it might seem difficult to discover this dominant characteristic. But to the student and lover of Hamilton it stands out clear and well-defined—Courage. Not the courage of the blind egoist or of the imperious politician, but the courage which had its roots in love of truth and of honorable dealing.

===Chinese===
亚历山大·汉密尔顿的热忱崇拜者当中，恐怕没有谁会对这个剧本完全满意。但两位作者聊以自慰地想：在区区一晚演出的限度之内，任何剧作家都无法公平展现这位非凡人物的魄力与格局。写作一部以伟大历史人物为主角的剧本，必须选取一件事，让它鲜明地凸显主人公最主要的特质。选定了这件事之后，明智的做法是不要再引入其他重要插曲把它搅得含混不清——无论那些插曲多么能为传主增光，也无论你多么忍不住想用它们。亚历山大·汉密尔顿在如此众多的领域卓有建树——当过航运账房职员，当过军人，是一位雄健而优雅的作家、一位演说家、一位战术家，还是国家金融政策的大师——以致随手翻翻史书的读者也许很难看出他那居于主导地位的特质。但在研究与热爱汉密尔顿的人看来，这一特质清晰而分明地凸现出来——勇气。不是盲目的利己者或专横的政客的那种勇气，而是植根于对真理与光明磊落之行事的热爱之中的勇气。

===Original===
And so the authors chose the incident which forms the basis of this play. In their opinion, no single event could be found that displays this fine quality of courage more surely and more definitely than the course adopted by Hamilton in the face of the attack by his political enemies. Those descendants of Alexander Hamilton whom the authors have had the honor of meeting, have expressed their satisfaction at the selection of this incident; and the authors feel that it is no breach of confidence to record that they have received words of praise from the two men who know more about Hamilton than perhaps anybody in America—two of his keenest admirers—Senator Lodge and Nicholas Murray Butler.

===Chinese===
于是，作者们选定了构成本剧基础的那件事。在他们看来，再没有哪一件事，能比汉密尔顿面对政敌攻击时所采取的方针更确凿、更分明地显示这种可贵的勇气了。作者们有幸会见过几位亚历山大·汉密尔顿的后人，他们都对选取这一事件表示满意；作者们也觉得，披露下面这件事并不算辜负什么人的信任——他们曾得到两位或许比全美国任何人都更了解汉密尔顿的人、也是他最热忱的两位崇拜者的称许：洛奇参议员与尼古拉斯·默里·巴特勒。

===Original===
The historical record on which the play is founded can be seen by any student who is so far interested, by applying to the Lenox Library in New York. It is known as the “Reynolds Pamphlet” and is the document written by Alexander Hamilton himself.

===Chinese===
本剧所依据的那份史料，凡有兴趣的读者均可向纽约的伦诺克斯图书馆申请查阅。它就是世称「雷诺兹小册子」的文献，出自亚历山大·汉密尔顿本人手笔。

===Original===
The play keeps very close to history. The main incidents are, in all essential details, historically correct. It has been necessary to take some few liberties but these are of minor importance. The dialogue is not written precisely as it might have been spoken at the end of the eighteenth century. The authors believed that a slavish attempt to eliminate all words and phrases that were probably not in vogue at the time would result in many instances in tedious phraseology and a certain artificiality, which they particularly desired to avoid. They have however endeavored on the whole to maintain the atmosphere of the period.

===Chinese===
本剧紧扣史实。主要情节在一切关键细节上均于史有据。有少数几处不得不有所变通，但都无关宏旨。剧中的对话并非严格照十八世纪末可能的口吻写成。作者们相信，若死板地非要剔除当时多半尚未流行的字眼与措辞，许多地方势必落入冗赘迂腐、矫揉造作——这正是他们特别想避免的。不过大体而言，他们仍力求保持那个时代的氛围。

===Original===
The stage directions are designed and intended for the guidance of the actors and not for the entertainment of the reader. There is a growing tendency amongst writers of plays to introduce long and humorous stage directions that are often very entertaining in the library but very dangerous and misleading for the stage. They are misleading to a producer because they frequently make a scene appear to be very sparkling, while it is in reality exceedingly dull—the sparkle being confined exclusively to the stage-directions. They are dangerous for the actor because they make him believe that his part is a great deal better than it really is, and so he is apt to regard his audience as stupid because their intelligence fails to appreciate subtleties that he detected at the reading. In reality it is the author who is to blame; he has let the actors into certain dark secrets connected with their characters, without giving them the ghost of an opportunity, through the dialogue or situation, of conveying these confidences to the audience.

===Chinese===
舞台说明是为指导演员而设计编写的，不是为了给读者解闷。剧作家当中渐有一种风气：喜欢写进冗长诙谐的舞台说明，这些说明在书斋里读来往往妙趣横生，搬上舞台却十分危险、误人不浅。它们误导制作人，因为常常让一场戏看上去火花四溅，实则沉闷之极——火花全都留在舞台说明里了。它们对演员也是危险的，因为会让演员以为自己的角色远比实际上高明，于是容易怪罪观众愚蠢，怪他们的智力领略不了他读剧本时察觉出的种种微妙之处。实际上，该受责备的是作者：他向演员们泄露了与角色相关的某些隐秘底细，却不给他们一丝一毫的机会，借对白或情境把这些底细传达给观众。

## Costumes / 服装表

===Original===
Act I

**Jefferson**. Cutaway coat of period, dark, heavy material brown corduroy breeches and vest; top boots to knees, plain leather, not polished; stock and plain fall; three-cornered black hat; wig as in plates or paintings.

**Monroe**. Dark green cutaway coat and breeches, light fancy vest; silk stockings, colonial shoes with buckles, stock and lace fall, three-cornered black hat; walking stick. Dark tie wig.

**Giles**. Light brown corduroy, long square cut coat, knee-breeches, snuff-colored plaid vest, stock and fall and hat. Dark tie wig.

**Tallyrand**. Cinnamon cutaway coat; silk vest, black silk stockings and white stripes, black silk knee-breeches, shoes; long black cane with tassel; large Napoleonic hat with black feather; white stock and elaborate lace fall; jewelry, chains, rings, etc. Auburn wig not tied.

**Schuyler**. Wine colored coat and breeches; dark green flowered vest, silk stockings and shoes; stock and lace fall; three-cornered beaver hat trimmed with silk ribbon; walking stick, white tie wig.

**Reynolds**. Prussian blue coat with tails; fancy flowered vest; buff colored riding breeches; top boots, plain leather; stock and fall; gray, three-cornered hat; dark tie wig.

**Melissa**. Buff colored skirt, three-quarters length; short armed, black bodice, laced down centre; fancy apron; shoes and stockings.

**Citizens and Quakers**. Costumes of period.

===Chinese===
第一幕

**杰斐逊**。当时样式的燕尾式外套，深色厚重料子；棕色灯芯绒马裤与背心；及膝长筒靴，素皮，不抛光；领巾配素色胸饰；黑色三角帽；假发照版画或油画中的样式。

**门罗**。深绿色燕尾式外套与马裤，浅色花哨背心；丝袜，带鞋扣的殖民地式鞋子，领巾与蕾丝胸饰，黑色三角帽；手杖。深色束发式假发。

**贾尔斯**。浅棕色灯芯绒，方襟长外套，及膝马裤，鼻烟色格纹背心，领巾、胸饰与帽子。深色束发式假发。

**塔列朗**。肉桂色燕尾式外套；丝绸背心，缀白条纹的黑丝袜，黑色丝质及膝马裤，鞋子；带流苏的黑色长手杖；缀黑羽的大号拿破仑式帽子；白色领巾与繁复的蕾丝胸饰；珠宝、链子、戒指等。赤褐色假发，散披不束。

**斯凯勒**。酒红色外套与马裤；深绿色绣花背心，丝袜与鞋子；领巾与蕾丝胸饰；缀丝带饰边的河狸皮三角帽；手杖，白色束发式假发。

**雷诺兹**。普鲁士蓝带尾外套；花哨的绣花背心；米黄色骑马马裤；素皮长筒靴；领巾与胸饰；灰色三角帽；深色束发式假发。

**梅利莎**。米黄色裙子，长及小腿；黑色短袖紧身胸衣，胸前当中系带收紧；花哨的围裙；鞋子与长袜。

**市民与贵格会教徒**。当时样式的服装。

===Original===
Act II

**Hamilton**. Light gray moire silk coat and knee-breeches, lightish green fancy vest; handsome stock and lace fall, gray silk stockings and colonial shoes with buckles. Medium fair tie wig.

**Schuyler**. As in Act I, with dark colonial cloak.

**Zekiel**. Snuff brown long square coat trimmed with black and steel buttons; breeches of same material; black stockings and shoes.

**Mrs. Whalen**. Long skirt, old dark material; short sleeved bodice of same; headdress, a piece of worn-out lace; shoes and stockings.

**Jefferson**. As in Act I.

**Monroe**. As in Act I.

**Tallyrand**. Rich champagne colored coat with dark red velvet collar; yellow silk breeches; fancy vest of yellow material with pattern; yellow silk stockings; dark shoes; long white cane with tassel; champagne gloves; hat as in Act I.

**Mrs. Reynolds**. Very low coat, cream colored gown of empire style without sleeves and of a very light filmy material with silk petticoat to match; stockings to match and shoes with straps across instep; dark blue cloak of light material with hood, lined with emerald green silk.

===Chinese===
第二幕

**汉密尔顿**。浅灰色云纹绸外套与及膝马裤，淡绿色花哨背心；讲究的领巾与蕾丝胸饰，灰色丝袜与带鞋扣的殖民地式鞋子。中等浅色的束发式假发。

**斯凯勒**。同第一幕，外加深色殖民地式斗篷。

**泽基尔**。鼻烟色方襟长外套，缀黑色与钢质纽扣；同料马裤；黑色长袜与鞋子。

**惠伦太太**。长裙，深色旧料子；同料短袖紧身胸衣；头上一方磨旧的蕾丝头饰；鞋子与长袜。

**杰斐逊**。同第一幕。

**门罗**。同第一幕。

**塔列朗**。华贵的香槟色外套，暗红丝绒领；黄色丝质马裤；带花纹的黄色料子花哨背心；黄色丝袜；深色鞋子；带流苏的白色长手杖；香槟色手套；帽子同第一幕。

**雷诺兹夫人**。领口极低的奶油色帝政式无袖长裙，料子极轻薄通透，配同色丝绸衬裙；同色长袜与足背绊带的鞋子；轻质料子的深蓝色带兜帽斗篷，翡翠绿绸里子。

===Original===
Act III

**Betsy Hamilton**. Pale pink dress of fine light silk, low cut and without sleeves; shoes and stockings.

**Angelica**. Street costume light brown silk with faint yellow plaid pattern; a dress of pale yellow voile which hangs in a panel down front; bodice with long sleeves and tulle trimming; large picture hat with tulle and marabou trimming; light blue silk shawl with black tassels and jet buttons on corners.

**Hamilton**. Old rose suit of fine silk; fancy vest with delicate pink pattern; black hat and cane.

**Jefferson**. Tight-fitting dark cutaway coat with knee-breeches and buff colored vest; black silk stockings and shoes; stock and lace fall and hat.

**Monroe**. Slate gray coat and knee-breeches; light fancy vest.

**Schuyler**. Dark green coat, flowered waistcoat; black silk breeches.

**Giles**. As in Act I.

**Judge Jay**. Blue coat and breeches, gray vest; blue stockings, shoes; stock and fall; gray tie wig.

===Chinese===
第三幕

**贝特西·汉密尔顿**。浅粉色细薄丝绸长裙，低领无袖；鞋子与长袜。

**安杰莉卡**。外出服为浅棕色丝绸，带隐约的黄色格纹；一幅浅黄色巴里纱垂挂在身前作长条饰片；长袖紧身胸衣，缀网眼纱花边；大号阔边装饰帽，饰以网眼纱与马罗羽毛；浅蓝色丝绸披肩，四角缀黑色流苏与黑玉纽扣。

**汉密尔顿**。暗玫瑰色细绸套装；缀淡雅粉色花纹的背心；黑色帽子与手杖。

**杰斐逊**。贴身的深色燕尾式外套，配及膝马裤与米黄色背心；黑色丝袜与鞋子；领巾、蕾丝胸饰与帽子。

**门罗**。石板灰外套与及膝马裤；浅色花哨背心。

**斯凯勒**。深绿色外套，绣花背心；黑色丝质马裤。

**贾尔斯**。同第一幕。

**杰伊法官**。蓝色外套与马裤，灰色背心；蓝色长袜，鞋子；领巾与胸饰；灰色束发式假发。

===Original===
Act IV

**Betsy**. Street costume of heavy dark blue silk with thin violet stripes; hat with feather, gloves and silk shawl.

**Angelica**. Rich gown of heavy yellow silk with brocaded buff showing a panel in front of pale violet tulle; short sleeves to elbow with lace trimmings.

**Hamilton**. Black velvet suit.

**Colonel Lear**. Military costume of period.

**Jefferson, Monroe, Schuyler and Jay**. As in Act III.

**Mrs. Reynolds**. Long trailing skirt of white messaline with polka dot pattern and red stripe; light gray tight fitting bodice with red tulle shawl; light green bonnet with rose in front on long stem; large feather muff.

===Chinese===
第四幕

**贝特西**。外出服为深蓝色厚绸，带纤细的紫色条纹；饰羽帽子、手套与丝绸披肩。

**安杰莉卡**。华贵的黄色厚绸长裙，米黄色锦纹，身前露出浅紫色网眼纱饰片；短袖及肘，缀蕾丝花边。

**汉密尔顿**。黑色丝绒套装。

**利尔上校**。当时样式的军装。

**杰斐逊、门罗、斯凯勒与杰伊**。同第三幕。

**雷诺兹夫人**。白色梅斯林软缎曳地长裙，带波点花纹与红色条纹；浅灰色贴身紧身胸衣，配红色网眼纱披巾；浅绿色软帽，前面簪一朵长茎玫瑰；大号羽毛手筒。
